## Isaiah 7:2 (id: 16732)

*hearts … trembled*: This means they were very afraid. In contrast, Ahaz’s son, King Hezekiah, faced the Assyrians about 30 years later with strong faith. In 701 BC, he trusted the Lord when Assyria threatened Judah (see [Isaiah 37:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa37:6-Isa37:7), [14–20](https://ref.ly/Isa37:14-Isa37:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tribe of Ephraim (ID: `group:Ephraim`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); David (ID: `person:David`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Isaiah 7:3 (id: 16733)

The aqueduct was a place where political negotiations took place later during Hezekiah’s reign (see [Isaiah 36:2](https://ref.ly/Isa36:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Shear-jashub (ID: `person:Shear-jashub`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`); Launderer’s Field (ID: `place:WashersField`); Ahaz (ID: `person:Ahaz`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 7:4 (id: 16734)

* *Do not be afraid*: This was a “do not fear” prophecy, which appears often in Isaiah. In these messages, the Lord assures his people that he is with them and will carry out his plan.
* *two smoldering stubs of firewood*: This image shows that Rezin and Pekah were weak threats. If Ahaz had trusted God’s view, he would have seen that they were about to disappear.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rezin (ID: `person:Rezin`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Remaliah (ID: `person:Remaliah`)

## Isaiah 7:6 (id: 16735)

The son of Tabeel*,* otherwise unknown, was obviously sympathetic to Israel’s and Syria’s resistance against Assyria.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tabeal (ID: `person:Tabeal`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`)

## Isaiah 7:8 (id: 16736)

* The Assyrian kings Esarhaddon and Ashurbanipal had Israel resettled with people from other places within 65years (by 670 BC; see [2 Kings 17:24–34](https://ref.ly/2Kgs17:24-2Kgs17:34)).
* Damascus was defeated and destroyed by 732 BC. Samaria was also defeated by 722 BC.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Damascus (ID: `place:Damascus`); Rezin (ID: `person:Rezin`); Tribe of Ephraim (ID: `group:Ephraim`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`)

## Isaiah 7:9 (id: 16737)

The last sentence is a play on two Hebrew words: If you do not stand firm (*te’amenu*) in your faith (*ta’aminu*), you will not stand at all. Firm trust in the Lord is essential, especially for a leader of God's people (see also [2 Chronicles 20:20](https://ref.ly/2Chr20:20)). Leaders must act on their faith to show it exists. 

Ahaz and the people of his time trusted Assyria, their enemy, instead of trusting God. They looked for help from a foreign power rather than from the Lord.

Later, King Hezekiah faced a similar threat. In that situation, he showed faith in the Lord (see [Isaiah 36–38](https://ref.ly/Isa36:1-Isa38:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Well-Established (ID: `keyterm:BeFirm`); Tribe of Ephraim (ID: `group:Ephraim`); Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`); Remaliah (ID: `person:Remaliah`); Believe In (ID: `keyterm:BelieveIn`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`)

## Isaiah 7:11 (id: 16722)

God would give Ahaz a sign to confirm his promise. This sign would take place before Ahaz’s eyes. It would show that God’s word was true.

Later, Ahaz’s son Hezekiah also received a sign from the Lord (see [Isaiah 37:30](https://ref.ly/Isa37:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)

## Isaiah 7:12 (id: 16723)

*I will not test the LORD*: Ahaz’s answer sounded respectful and faithful (based on [Deuteronomy 6:16\.](https://ref.ly/Deut6:16) However, Ahaz was likely already speaking with the Assyrians. He had probably already decided to trust them for help in this war. His response sounded spiritual and proper, but his actions showed that he did not plan to trust the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ahaz (ID: `person:Ahaz`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 7:14 (id: 16724)

* This prophecy was fully realized with the birth of Jesus Christ ([Matthew 1:18–24](https://ref.ly/Matt1:18-Matt1:24)). It is likely that it also partially came true in Isaiah's time, possibly with the birth of the godly King Hezekiah, son of Ahaz, or one of Isaiah's children. The similar verb sequence in [Isaiah 7:14](https://ref.ly/Isa7:14) and [8:3](https://ref.ly/Isa8:3) (*be with child* … *give birth* … *call*) and the connection between Immanuel and Maher\-shalal\-hash\-baz in [8:5–10](https://ref.ly/Isa8:5-Isa8:10) suggest they were the same person.
* The name *Immanuel* means "God with us." This name symbolized God's presence and protection. God was with Judah:

    + during the attack by the alliance of Syria and Israel in 734 BC,
        + during the Assyrian crisis in 701 BC, and
        + throughout their existence until their fall in 586 BC.
        The kingdom of Israel fell during Isaiah’s lifetime, in 722 BC.

    Even after that disaster, the promise “I am with you” remained important. God spoke these words to his people during the exile and after they returned home ([Isaiah 41:10](https://ref.ly/Isa41:10); [43:2](https://ref.ly/Isa43:2), [5](https://ref.ly/Isa43:5)).

    The fullest meaning of this promise appears in Jesus Christ, the incarnate Son of God. *Incarnate* means that God took on human flesh. Jesus is called *Immanuel* ([Matthew 1:23](https://ref.ly/Matt1:23); see also [Revelation 12:5](https://ref.ly/Rev12:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Isaiah 7:15–16 (id: 16725)

* *By the time He knows enough*: The crisis with the alliance of Syria and Israel against Judah occurred in 734 BC. By 732 BC, Damascus, the capital of Syria, was destroyed. Israel, the northern kingdom, was also defeated.
* *to reject evil and choose good*: This was to happen by age 12, when a child was considered an adult. In 722 BC, Israel went into exile.
* *He will be eating curds and honey*: The land would lose so many people that food would become easy to find. Even rich foods, once rare, would be available to everyone.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:15-16


## Isaiah 7:17 (id: 16726)

* Israel separated from Judah in 931 BC (see [1 Kings 12:19–20](https://ref.ly/1Kgs12:19-1Kgs12:20)).
* *He will bring the king of Assyria*: Ahaz asked Assyria for help ([2 Kings 16:8–9](https://ref.ly/2Kgs16:8-2Kgs16:9)). However, Assyria later turned against him. Judah became a vassal state, which means it was forced to serve and pay tribute to Assyria.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tribe of Ephraim (ID: `group:Ephraim`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`)

## Isaiah 7:18–25 (id: 16727)

The phrase *on that day* refers to the future Day of the LORD, when God acts to judge and to save. 

Judah would first experience the judgment Isaiah had announced. This would be a warning of a greater judgment still to come. That greater judgment was the exile in Babylon from 586 to 538 BC.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:18-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`); Hoe (ID: `realia:Hoe`); Fly (ID: `fauna:Fly`); Razor (ID: `realia:Razor`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Arrow (ID: `realia:Arrow`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`); Bee (ID: `fauna:Bee`)

## Isaiah 7:19 (id: 16728)

Desolate valleys, caves, and thorny areas were often places where people hid in times of danger (see [Isaiah 2:10](https://ref.ly/Isa2:10)). People who were afraid would run there for safety.

However, Judah’s enemies would still find them. These hiding places would not protect them from judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`)

## Isaiah 7:20 (id: 16730)

*shave your head and the hair of your legs, and to remove your beard as well*: In the ancient Near East, forced shaving was a disgraceful act (see [2 Samuel 10:4–5](https://ref.ly/2Sam10:4-2Sam10:5)). Here, it served as a metaphor for the destruction of the country.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Razor (ID: `realia:Razor`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`)

## Isaiah 7:23–25 (id: 16731)

Farmers faced almost complete disaster. Fertile fields and lush vineyards would turn into wild grazing lands filled with useless plants like briers and thorns (see [Isaiah 5:6](https://ref.ly/Isa5:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 7:23-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`); Hoe (ID: `realia:Hoe`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Arrow (ID: `keyterm:Arrow`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Arrow (ID: `realia:Arrow`)

## Isaiah 8:1 (id: 16748)

The name *Maher\-shalal\-hash\-baz* means “Swift to plunder and quick to carry away.” This name applies to both Judah's enemies ([Isaiah 8:4](https://ref.ly/Isa8:4)) and Judah itself ([8:7–8](https://ref.ly/Isa8:7-Isa8:8)). Judah trusted Assyria instead of God, and now Assyria would turn against them and nearly destroy them. God was with them (*Immanuel;* see [Isaiah 7:14](https://ref.ly/Isa7:14)), but his presence would be harmful if they refused to trust him (see [8:14](https://ref.ly/Isa8:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Maher-shalal-hash-baz (ID: `person:Maher-shalal-hash-baz`); Stylus (ID: `realia:Stylus`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tablet (ID: `realia:Tablet`)

## Isaiah 8:3 (id: 16750)

Like the name *Immanuel* ([Isaiah 7:14](https://ref.ly/Isa7:14)), Maher\-shalal\-hash\-baz is symbolic. This child might have partially fulfilled the Immanuel prophecy (see study note on Isaiah 7:14). The ultimate fulfillment of the Immanuel prophecy happened with the birth of Jesus ([Matthew 1:22–23](https://ref.ly/Matt1:22-Matt1:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:3
* **Associated Articles:** Isaiah 7:14 (ID: `16724@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Maher-shalal-hash-baz (ID: `person:Maher-shalal-hash-baz`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 8:4 (id: 16751)

* *before the boy knows how to cry*: See also [Isaiah 7:16](https://ref.ly/Isa7:16).
* Damascus and Samaria were ruined and destroyed in 732 BC. This event fulfilled this verse along with [Isaiah 7:8](https://ref.ly/Isa7:8), [19–20](https://ref.ly/Isa7:19-Isa7:20).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Damascus (ID: `place:Damascus`); Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`)

## Isaiah 8:5–10 (id: 16752)

This prophecy connects the sign of Maher\-shalal\-hash\-baz with the sign of Immanuel ([Isaiah 8:8](https://ref.ly/Isa8:8), [10](https://ref.ly/Isa8:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:5-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Rezin (ID: `person:Rezin`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Siloam (ID: `place:Siloam`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Remaliah (ID: `person:Remaliah`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`)

## Isaiah 8:6 (id: 16753)

* The gently flowing waters of Shiloah provided Jerusalem with water. They symbolize God's loving presence and care (see [Psalm 46:4](https://ref.ly/Ps46:4)).
* Ahaz rejected God's sign and promise of protection. He lacked faith ([Isaiah 7:9](https://ref.ly/Isa7:9)). Now the people followed his example by hoping Assyria would save them from King Rezin and King Pekah (compare [Isaiah 7:7–9](https://ref.ly/Isa7:7-Isa7:9), [16](https://ref.ly/Isa7:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rezin (ID: `person:Rezin`); Remaliah (ID: `person:Remaliah`); Siloam (ID: `place:Siloam`)

## Isaiah 8:7 (id: 16754)

The mighty flood from the Euphrates River symbolizes the invading Assyrian army (see [Jeremiah 47:2](https://ref.ly/Jer47:2)). The river would overflow its channels when the Assyrians advanced into Judah. This was a stark contrast to the gently flowing waters of Shiloah ([Isaiah 8:6](https://ref.ly/Isa8:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`)

## Isaiah 8:8 (id: 16755)

* *up to the neck*: Judah barely survived the Assyrian attacks in 701 BC (see [Isaiah 36–37](https://ref.ly/Isa36:1-Isa37:38)).
* *its spreading streams will cover your entire land*: From north to south, Judah was almost destroyed, except for Jerusalem (see [Isaiah 1:8–9](https://ref.ly/Isa1:8-Isa1:9); chapters [36–37](https://ref.ly/Isa36:1-Isa37:38)).
* *Immanuel*: Despite their lack of trust and the resulting disaster, God remained with his people (see [Isaiah 7:14](https://ref.ly/Isa7:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Isaiah 8:9–10 (id: 16756)

God's plan to save his stubborn people would eventually succeed. God intended to destroy Assyria, just as he does with any proud nation that rejects him or brags about its own power.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:9-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`)

## Isaiah 8:10 (id: 16738)

*God is with us*: See [Isaiah 7:14](https://ref.ly/Isa7:14) and [8:8](https://ref.ly/Isa8:8).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`)

## Isaiah 8:11–15 (id: 16739)

This text reveals Isaiah's main message. The issue was fear of the Lord versus fear of people (see [Isaiah 7:9](https://ref.ly/Isa7:9); [8:6](https://ref.ly/Isa8:6)). When someone fears people, the Lord becomes a trap and destruction is certain (compare [Proverbs 29:25](https://ref.ly/Prov29:25); see “Fearing People” Theme Note). For those who fear the Lord, he becomes a sanctuary and their salvation is assured.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:11-15
* **Associated Articles:** Fearing People (ID: `500291@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 8:12 (id: 16740)

People saw Isaiah’s message of staying out of Assyrian affairs as treason and part of a conspiracy. However, Isaiah was not engaging in political games. He was sharing God’s message. 

What scared them was the alliance of Syria and Israel attacking Judah (see [Isaiah 7:1](https://ref.ly/Isa7:1), [4](https://ref.ly/Isa7:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Isaiah 8:13 (id: 16741)

*you shall regard as holy*: This means to give God first place in life and recognize that nothing is greater than he is. 

*He should be feared*: This means honoring God with deep respect ([Proverbs 1:7](https://ref.ly/Prov1:7)). The people of Judah were not to fear Israel and Syria or the Assyrians ([Isaiah 7:9](https://ref.ly/Isa7:9); [10:24](https://ref.ly/Isa10:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 8:14 (id: 16742)

* H*e will be a sanctuary*: God is a place of refuge.
* *stone … stumbling … rock … offense*: By trusting Assyria instead of God, the people of Jerusalem would fall into a trap as the Assyrians turned against them. However, for those who trust in the Lord, he is faithful and strong ([Isaiah 28:16](https://ref.ly/Isa28:16); see [Psalm 118:22–23](https://ref.ly/Ps118:22-Ps118:23); [Luke 2:34](https://ref.ly/Luke2:34); [Romans 9:32–33](https://ref.ly/Rom9:32-Rom9:33); [1 Peter 2:6–8](https://ref.ly/1Pet2:6-1Pet2:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Isaiah 8:16 (id: 16744)

*law* (Hebrew *torah*): The Torah described how Israel was to live in covenant with God. However, the people quickly forgot these instructions (see also [Isaiah 1:10](https://ref.ly/Isa1:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`)

## Isaiah 8:16–17 (id: 16743)

The command to preserve Isaiah’s teaching suggests that many people did not accept his message. Some may have wanted to silence him and stop his words from spreading.

However, faithful disciples kept his messages safe. They protected and passed on the prophet’s words to future generations.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:16-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`)

## Isaiah 8:17 (id: 16745)

* *I will wait for the LORD … I will put my trust in Him*: Isaiah’s message about Syria, Israel, and Assyria came true in later historical events. This fulfillment encouraged the faithful to wait for the end of all unjust power structures (see [Isaiah 8:9–10](https://ref.ly/Isa8:9-Isa8:10)).

    Waiting for the Lord involves submission, prayer, hope, and faith (see [Isaiah 25:9](https://ref.ly/Isa25:9); [26:8](https://ref.ly/Isa26:8); [33:2](https://ref.ly/Isa33:2)). This leads to a calm spirit and renewed inner strength ([40:31](https://ref.ly/Isa40:31)).

    The book of Hebrews connects these words to Jesus Christ ([Hebrews 2:13](https://ref.ly/Heb2:13)).

* God had turned away. The people of Israel and Judah were separated from the Lord because of their sin.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 8:18 (id: 16746)

*Here am I, and the children the LORD has given me as* (see the reference to Jesus Christ in [Hebrews 2:13](https://ref.ly/Heb2:13)): The names of the prophet Isaiah and his children held important meanings as signs and warnings. 

Shear\-jashub (*a remnant will return;* [Isaiah 7:3](https://ref.ly/Isa7:3)) symbolized God's faithfulness to Judah. 

Maher\-shalal\-hash\-baz (*swift to plunder and quick to carry away*) indicated the destruction of Damascus and Samaria ([Isaiah 8:1–4](https://ref.ly/Isa8:1-Isa8:4)) and the desolation of unfaithful Judah ([8:5–8](https://ref.ly/Isa8:5-Isa8:8)). 

The name of Isaiah (*Yahweh is salvation*) itself signifies that salvation is from the Lord alone. These three names also represent major themes in the book: 

* the remnant (the small group of people who remain faithful),
* the coming desolation of Judah (the land becoming empty and ruined), and
* salvation (God’s rescue of his people).

Immanuel (*God is with us*) brings together all of these themes. It also shows that Judah would be protected if the people trusted in the Lord. God’s presence with them was their true safety.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Isaiah 8:19–22 (id: 16747)

* Isaiah contrasted his message with the advice of ungodly people around him. His message brought light and truth. The message of spiritists led to darkness and death.
* Mediums tried to discover the future through divination (seeking hidden knowledge through rituals). Some claimed to speak with the dead (see [Isaiah 19:3](https://ref.ly/Isa19:3)). However, God had forbidden these practices because they were empty and harmful ([Isaiah 47:9](https://ref.ly/Isa47:9); [Deuteronomy 18:9–11](https://ref.ly/Deut18:9-Deut18:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:19-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Necromancer (ID: `keyterm:Spirit`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`)

God gave instructions and teachings through the law and through his prophets, such as Isaiah (see [Isaiah 8:16](https://ref.ly/Isa8:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 8:20

## Isaiah 9:1 (id: 16763)

* Zebulun and Naphtali were northern tribes in Israel ([Judges 6:35](https://ref.ly/Judg6:35)). The Assyrian invaders, led by Tiglath\-pileser III, humbled them in 734 and 732 BC. During the lifetime of Jesus, people connected this prophecy to his coming out from Galilee ([Matthew 4:14–16](https://ref.ly/Matt4:14-Matt4:16)).
* Galilee of the Gentiles was the area of Israel between the Sea of Galilee and the Mediterranean Sea. The surrounding Gentile nations heavily influenced it culturally, religiously, and politically.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 9:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tribe of Zebulun (ID: `group:Zebulun`); Naphtali (ID: `person:Naphtali`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Zebulun (ID: `person:Zebulun`); Tribe of Naphtali (ID: `group:Naphtali`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`)

## Isaiah 9:1–7 (id: 16757)

After judgment and destruction, there would still be hope. God promised that the Messiah (God’s chosen one) would come and rule.

In the Messiah’s kingdom, darkness would end and great light would begin. This light would first appear in Galilee. Then it would spread to the whole nation of Israel and finally to the whole world.

The Messiah would defend those who suffer and are treated unfairly. He would bring an end to cruelty and injustice. He would rule with justice (what is right and fair) and righteousness (living in a right way before God). His kingdom would be a kingdom of peace, and it would last forever.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 9:1-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Tribe of Zebulun (ID: `group:Zebulun`); Naphtali (ID: `person:Naphtali`); Zebulun (ID: `person:Zebulun`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); David (ID: `person:David`); Tribe of Naphtali (ID: `group:Naphtali`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Midian (ID: `place:Midian`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Yoke (ID: `realia:Yoke`)

## Isaiah 9:4 (id: 16765)

* The people experienced the rule of other nations as a yoke of slavery and a heavy burden. A yoke is a wooden bar placed on animals to control them. Here it describes harsh control and forced labor ([Jeremiah 30:8](https://ref.ly/Jer30:8)).
* *the rod of their oppressor*: In [Isaiah 10:5](https://ref.ly/Isa10:5), the Assyrians are called “the rod of \[the Lord’s] anger.” This means God used Assyria to punish his people. But God’s anger would not last forever. When his anger ended, Assyria’s cruel rule over Judah would also end ([Isaiah 14:5](https://ref.ly/Isa14:5)).
* The Lord had destroyed the army of Midian through Gideon (see [Judges 6:35](https://ref.ly/Judg6:35); [7:22–25](https://ref.ly/Judg7:22-Judg7:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 9:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Midian (ID: `place:Midian`); Yoke (ID: `realia:Yoke`)

## Isaiah 9:5 (id: 16766)

Burning the boots and uniforms marked the end of the need for instruments of war.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 9:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`)

## Isaiah 9:6 (id: 16767)

* *unto us a child is born, unto us a son is given*: This child, the Messiah, would be David’s descendant ([Isaiah 11:1](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1)).
* *He will be called*: These titles describe the kind of king this child would be. They can be understood as four royal names that show the nature of his rule:

    1. The *Wonderful Counselor* rules with God’s wisdom ([Isaiah 11:1](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1); [25:1](https://ref.ly/Isa25:1); [28:29](https://ref.ly/Isa28:29); [40:13](https://ref.ly/Isa40:13)). His wisdom is different from the weak and foolish leaders of Judah ([1:26](https://ref.ly/Isa1:26); [3:3](https://ref.ly/Isa3:3)).
        2. The *Mighty God* is an affirmation of the Messiah’s divine nature. It shows that he shares in God’s power and authority.
        3. The *Everlasting Father* cares for his people like a father. His care never ends (compare [Isaiah 22:21](https://ref.ly/Isa22:21); [63:16](https://ref.ly/Isa63:16)).
        4. The *Prince of Peace* is a ruler who brings peace, not war.
* Some scholars understand the titles as two longer names instead of four:

    1. “A Wonderful Counselor \[is] the Mighty God”; and
        2. “the Everlasting Father \[is] a Prince of Peace” (on his nature, see [Isaiah 11:1–9](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:9)).
* The New Testament identifies Jesus Christ as this promised Son of David ([Matthew 1:1](https://ref.ly/Matt1:1); [Luke 1:32](https://ref.ly/Luke1:32); see also [Isaiah 7:14](https://ref.ly/Isa7:14); [8:3](https://ref.ly/Isa8:3), [18](https://ref.ly/Isa8:18)). He will establish his kingdom of peace ([Revelation 19](https://ref.ly/Rev19:1-Rev19:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 9:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 9:7 (id: 16768)

This ruler, like Ahaz, will be a descendant of David. However, unlike Ahaz and the rulers of Jerusalem ([Isaiah 1:21–23](https://ref.ly/Isa1:21-Isa1:23)), he will trust God and rule with justice and righteousness ([11:1–3](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:3)). Jesus the Messiah reigns forever ([Luke 1:32–33](https://ref.ly/Luke1:32-Luke1:33)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 9:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); David (ID: `person:David`)

## Isaiah 9:13–17 (id: 16760)

God used punishment to call his people to repentance. Repentance means turning away from sin and turning back to God. His goal was not to destroy Israel, but to correct them and bring them back to himself.

However, Israel had become apostate. This means they had turned away from the Lord and rejected him. They refused to repent or to seek the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 9:13-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Foolish (ID: `keyterm:Foolish`); Palm Branch and Reed (ID: `keyterm:PalmBranchAndReed`); Date Palm (ID: `flora:DatePalm`)

## Isaiah 9:17 (id: 16761)

Widows and orphans are usually special objects of God’s compassion and care ([Isaiah 1:17](https://ref.ly/Isa1:17), [23](https://ref.ly/Isa1:23); [10:2](https://ref.ly/Isa10:2)). However, when they remained unrepentant, they also faced God’s wrath. This means they experienced his judgment. God’s judgment was not based on social position, but on whether people turned back to him.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 9:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Foolish (ID: `keyterm:Foolish`)

## Isaiah 9:18–21 (id: 16762)

After the leaders were destroyed, the nation would fall into chaos. With no strong leadership, people would turn against one another.

Civil war would then destroy the nation. It would spread quickly and cause great damage, like a brushfire that no one can control.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 9:18-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Ephraim (ID: `group:Ephraim`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Tribe of Manasseh (ID: `group:Manasseh`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`)

## Isaiah 9:21 (id: 16764)

Manasseh and Ephraim represent the northern kingdom because they were its largest tribes. These tribes stood for the whole kingdom of Israel.

The two largest tribes of Israel and Judah fought against each other. This likely refers to the war between Judah and the alliance of Syria and Israel. This war is often called the Syro\-Ephraimite War ([Isaiah 7:1–12](https://ref.ly/Isa7:1-Isa7:12)). During this conflict, people from the same family fought against one another. In other words, brothers fought against brothers ([2 Chronicles 28](https://ref.ly/2Chr28:1-2Chr28:27)). This civil war brought great suffering to God’s people.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 9:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tribe of Ephraim (ID: `group:Ephraim`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Tribe of Manasseh (ID: `group:Manasseh`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`)

## Isaiah 10:1–4 (id: 15815)

This prophecy was spoken against the leaders of Judah. They used their positions of authority for personal gain.

Instead of serving the people, they made themselves rich. They did this at the people’s expense. God spoke this message to confront their injustice and wrongdoing.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); Visitation (ID: `keyterm:Visitation`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`)

## Isaiah 10:3 (id: 15831)

* Isaiah asked several questions, but he did not expect an answer. The implied answer was clear. When God punished Judah, there would be no one to help them.
* Disaster first came from the distant land of Assyria. Later, it came from Babylon. These nations became the instruments of God’s judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Visitation (ID: `keyterm:Visitation`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Isaiah 10:5 (id: 15834)

The Assyrians were God’s instrument in judging Syria, Israel, and Judah. The prophet describes them as a *rod* and a *club*. These are tools used to strike or discipline someone.

God used Assyria to punish these nations. However, Assyria acted with pride and cruelty. Because of their own wickedness, they would not escape judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`)

## Isaiah 10:5–19 (id: 15833)

God announced judgment on Assyria for several reasons: 

* They destroyed other nations without mercy ([Isaiah 10:7](https://ref.ly/Isa10:7)).
* They spoke with pride and dishonored God. They boasted about their own power ([Isaiah 10:12](https://ref.ly/Isa10:12)).
* They oppressed other peoples and took what did not belong to them ([Isaiah 10:13–14](https://ref.ly/Isa10:13-Isa10:14)).
* They believed they were acting on their own. They did not recognize that God was using them ([Isaiah 10:15](https://ref.ly/Isa10:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:5-19

## Isaiah 10:5–11.16 (id: 15832)

[Isaiah 9:8–10:4](https://ref.ly/Isa9:8-Isa10:4) shows that the Lord’s moral law is supreme. This means God’s standards of right and wrong stand above every nation and ruler. The next section explains what this truth means: 

1. Assyria was only a tool in God’s hands. Because of this, Assyria was also responsible to God. Like every other nation, it would face judgment for its sin ([Isaiah 10:5–19](https://ref.ly/Isa10:5-Isa10:19), [28–34](https://ref.ly/Isa10:28-Isa10:34)).
2. Those among God’s people who trusted in him and obeyed his covenant would be rescued ([Isaiah 10:20–27](https://ref.ly/Isa10:20-Isa10:27)).
3. God would establish his kingdom on earth ([Isaiah 11](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:5-11:16

## Isaiah 10:6 (id: 15835)

* Judah had a special calling as God’s covenant people. A covenant is a binding agreement between God and his people. However, Judah turned away from the Lord and became a godless nation.

    Because of this, they became the object of God’s anger ([Isaiah 10:4](https://ref.ly/Isa10:4)). God’s judgment came not because he broke his covenant, but because they did.

* The word *plunder* translates two Hebrew words, *shalal* and *baz*. Both words mean to take goods by force after victory in battle.

    These words recall the name *Maher\-shalal\-hash\-baz* (see [Isaiah 5:19](https://ref.ly/Isa5:19); [8:1](https://ref.ly/Isa8:1), [3](https://ref.ly/Isa8:3), [5–10](https://ref.ly/Isa8:5-Isa8:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:6


## Isaiah 10:7 (id: 15836)

The Assyrians did not understand that they were carrying out God’s purpose. God used them to punish Israel and Judah.

However, the Assyrians acted on their own desires. They killed and looted other nations with cruelty. They did more than God intended.

Because of their pride and violence, God would hold them guilty. They would also face his judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:7


## Isaiah 10:9 (id: 15837)

By 717 BC, Assyria controlled the regions north of Judah. These areas had fallen one by one.

Calno was a city in northern Syria. It is called Calneh in [Amos 6:2\.](https://ref.ly/Amos6:2) The Assyrian king Tiglath\-pileser III captured it in 738 BC.

Carchemish stood on the Euphrates River. King Sargon II captured it in 717 BC.

Hamath was located on the Orontes River. Assyria brought it under control in 738 BC and again in 720 BC.

Arpad was a city south of Calno. Damascus and Samaria were the capital cities of Syria and Israel. Assyria captured Damascus in 732 BC and Samaria in 722 BC ([Isaiah 7:8](https://ref.ly/Isa7:8); [8:4](https://ref.ly/Isa8:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Carchemish (ID: `place:Carchemish`); Calneh (ID: `place:Calneh`); Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`); Hamath (ID: `place:Hamath`); Arpad (ID: `place:Arpad`); Damascus (ID: `place:Damascus`)

*whose images surpassed those of Jerusalem and Samaria*: Assyria would make this argument while threatening Jerusalem ([Isaiah 36:19–20](https://ref.ly/Isa36:19-Isa36:20); [37:12](https://ref.ly/Isa37:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Idol (ID: `keyterm:Idol`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Isaiah 10:11 (id: 15817)

Assyria destroyed Samaria in 722 BC. This happened during the reigns of Shalmaneser V and Sargon II ([2 Kings 17:3–6](https://ref.ly/2Kgs17:3-2Kgs17:6)). 

Samaria and Judah both worshiped the same God. Because Assyria had already defeated Samaria, the king of Assyria believed he could also defeat Judah.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Idol (ID: `keyterm:Idol`); Samaria (ID: `place:Samaria`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Isaiah 10:12 (id: 15818)

Any royal power that exalts itself against the Lord is proud and arrogant. The Lord is the Great King over all rulers.

When kings lift themselves up against him, they set themselves in opposition to God. Such pride will not last.

God will humble and crush every ruler who stands against him (see [Isaiah 2:11–12](https://ref.ly/Isa2:11-Isa2:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Isaiah 10:13 (id: 15819)

*By the strength of my hand … and by my wisdom*(compare [Isaiah 10:5](https://ref.ly/Isa10:5)): Only God is all\-powerful and wise. He plans the future and decides which nations will rise to power and which will be defeated.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Mighty One (ID: `keyterm:MightyOne`)

## Isaiah 10:15 (id: 15820)

*axe … saw … rod … staff*: These tools are only as effective as the person using them. Assyria thought it was independent of God's influence. However, their victories were possible only with his permission and to fulfill his purposes ([Isaiah 10:5](https://ref.ly/Isa10:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`); Saw (ID: `realia:Saw`); Axe (ID: `realia:Axe`); Staff (ID: `realia:Staff`)

## Isaiah 10:16 (id: 15821)

This prophecy was fulfilled in 701 BC. In that year, 185,000 Assyrian soldiers died.

They were struck down by a plague and by flaming fire. This event marked a sudden and dramatic defeat for Assyria (see [Isaiah 37:36](https://ref.ly/Isa37:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 10:20 (id: 15824)

*the survivors*: This refers to a small group who remained alive. This group is called a remnant. A remnant is a small number of people who survive after great destruction.

A remnant from Jerusalem barely survived. However, their safety was secure because God had promised to preserve them (see [Isaiah 1:8–9](https://ref.ly/Isa1:8-Isa1:9); [4:2](https://ref.ly/Isa4:2); [6:13](https://ref.ly/Isa6:13); [37:31–32](https://ref.ly/Isa37:31-Isa37:32)). 

These wars taught the remnant an important lesson. They were not to depend on powerful nations such as Assyria for help.

Instead, they were to trust faithfully in the Lord alone (see [Isaiah 8:13](https://ref.ly/Isa8:13); [17:7](https://ref.ly/Isa17:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 10:20–27 (id: 15823)

In the middle of Assyrian oppression, there was still hope for the remnant. This passage explains more clearly the meaning of the name *Shear\-jashub*. The name means “a remnant will return” ([Isaiah 10:21](https://ref.ly/Isa10:21); see [7:3](https://ref.ly/Isa7:3); [8:18](https://ref.ly/Isa8:18)).

The promise showed that God would not destroy all his people. Some would return to him and live under his care.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:20-27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`); Oreb (ID: `person:Oreb`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Assyrians (ID: `group:Assyria`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Midian (ID: `place:Midian`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Staff (ID: `realia:Staff`); Whip (ID: `realia:Whip`); Yoke (ID: `realia:Yoke`)

## Isaiah 10:22 (id: 15825)

* God had promised Abraham that his descendants would be as numerous as the sand on the seashore ([Genesis 22:17](https://ref.ly/Gen22:17)).
* *Destruction has been decreed, overflowing with righteousness*: God's decision was right and just. It was a response to the people’s continued and stubborn wickedness. God’s judgment did not cancel his promise. He remained faithful, even while he punished sin.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

*as the Egyptians did*: See [Exodus 1:8–2:25](https://ref.ly/Exod1:8-Exod2:25).

## Isaiah 10:27 (id: 15829)

Israel’s captivity ended in 539 BC. At that time, many of the people returned from exile. This return is sometimes described as a new exodus because God brought them out of a foreign land again.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Yoke (ID: `realia:Yoke`)

## Isaiah 10:28–34 (id: 15830)

*Assyria has entered Aiath*: [Isaiah 10:29–32](https://ref.ly/Isa10:29-Isa10:32) describes the march of the Assyrian army in poetic language. The cities are listed in order from the north toward Jerusalem.

The army moves closer and closer to the city. When they reach Jerusalem, they are forced to stop and retreat.

God was with Jerusalem. Because of this, Assyria would not succeed. In time, Assyria itself would be destroyed.

This passage may refer to Sennacherib’s invasion in 701 BC (see [Isaiah 36–37](https://ref.ly/Isa36:1-Isa37:38)). However, it could also describe an earlier or later invasion.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 10:28-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Nob (ID: `place:Nob`); Madmenah (ID: `place:Madmenah`); Migron (ID: `place:Migron`); Gebim (ID: `place:Gebim`); Gallim (ID: `place:Gallim`); Ai (ID: `place:Ai`); Laishah (ID: `place:Laishah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); Michmash (ID: `place:Michmash`); Saul (ID: `person:Saul.2`); Gibeah (ID: `place:Gibeah.2`); Anathoth (ID: `place:Anathoth`); Ramah (ID: `place:Ramah.2`); Mighty (ID: `keyterm:Mighty`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Geba (ID: `place:Geba`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Isaiah 11:1 (id: 15844)

*shoot … stump … Branch from his roots*: This new growth represents the continuation of David's royal family line, even after it almost ended during the exile in Babylon (see also [Isaiah 6:13](https://ref.ly/Isa6:13); [39:7](https://ref.ly/Isa39:7); [Rev 22:16](https://ref.ly/Rev22:16)). This new growth from the old roots will not resemble the previous weak and unjust descendants of David (see [Isaiah 4:2](https://ref.ly/Isa4:2)). Jesus, the Messiah (God's chosen one), is the final fulfillment.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 11:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesse (ID: `person:Jesse`)

## Isaiah 11:1–16 (id: 15838)

The kingdom of the Messiah, first mentioned in [Isaiah 9:1–7](https://ref.ly/Isa9:1-Isa9:7), is now explored in more detail. Isaiah questioned the Israelites about their trust in other nations when God had far better plans in mind.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 11:1-16

## Isaiah 11:2 (id: 15845)

* The Spirit of the Lord will rest on the Messiah. The Spirit ensures David's line continues. The Messiah's arrival will bring:

    + justice and righteousness ([Isaiah 11:4](https://ref.ly/Isa11:4)),
        + peace ([Isaiah 11:8](https://ref.ly/Isa11:8)), and
        + the extension of God's kingdom to all nations ([Isaiah 11:10](https://ref.ly/Isa11:10); see also [32:15–16](https://ref.ly/Isa32:15-Isa32:16)).
* The promised Messiah will have great wisdom and understanding, like King Solomon. He will also have knowledge and reverence for the Lord ([1 Kings 3:28](https://ref.ly/1Kgs3:28); [4:29](https://ref.ly/1Kgs4:29); [Proverbs 1:1–7](https://ref.ly/Prov1:1-Prov1:7); [2:6–7](https://ref.ly/Prov2:6-Prov2:7)).
* The *Spirit of counsel and strength* refers to [Isaiah 9:6](https://ref.ly/Isa9:6). The Messiah will be wise and have the power to rule righteously.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 11:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`)

## Isaiah 11:3 (id: 15846)

*the fear of the LORD*: This means having deep respect and reverence for the Lord. It does not mean being afraid in a frightened way (see also [Isaiah 11:2](https://ref.ly/Isa11:2) and [Proverbs 1:7](https://ref.ly/Prov1:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 11:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Isaiah 11:4 (id: 15847)

* The poor and the exploited would receive the justice due to them, which the wicked leaders of Judah had previously withheld.
* *the rod of his mouth*: See [Psalm 2:9](https://ref.ly/Ps2:9); [Revelation 19:15](https://ref.ly/Rev19:15).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 11:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`); Poor (ID: `keyterm:Poor`)

## Isaiah 11:5 (id: 15848)

Because the Messiah is righteous in his character, his rule will show justice, righteousness, and truth. These qualities will mark his reign.

This will be very different from the condition of Jerusalem described in [Isaiah 1:21](https://ref.ly/Isa1:21). There, the city is described as unjust and unfaithful.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 11:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Belt (ID: `realia:Belt`)

## Isaiah 11:6 (id: 15849)

This prophecy was partly fulfilled during Isaiah's time or soon after. The complete fulfillment will occur when the Messiah (God's chosen one) arrives to begin and fully establish the age to come.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 11:6

## Isaiah 11:10 (id: 15839)

* The Messiah will bring salvation to the entire world ([Isaiah 2:1–5](https://ref.ly/Isa2:1-Isa2:5)). People from other nations will serve as priests and Levites ([Isaiah 66:20–21](https://ref.ly/Isa66:20-Isa66:21)), but only if they repent and believe in the true God.

## Isaiah 11:11 (id: 15840)

* The first time the Lord reached out his hand was during the exodus. The second time will be in the future when the Messiah gathers non\-Jewish people ([Isaiah 11:10](https://ref.ly/Isa11:10)) and his own people living among them ([11:11](https://ref.ly/Isa11:11)).

    The exodus remained a symbol of hope for those in exile (see [Isaiah 11:16](https://ref.ly/Isa11:16); [35:8](https://ref.ly/Isa35:8); [40:3](https://ref.ly/Isa40:3); [43:19–20](https://ref.ly/Isa43:19-Isa43:20); [48:21](https://ref.ly/Isa48:21)).

* The remnant of his people included Israelites who survived the Assyrian and Babylonian invasions and were exiled.

## Isaiah 12:1–6 (id: 15851)

The prophet's personal song of praise for God's salvation concludes the reflections on trust in [Isaiah 7–12](https://ref.ly/Isa7:1-Isa12:6). God will show he is trustworthy in his promises to rescue his people, even though they initially refused to trust him. The song anticipates [Isaiah 40–66](https://ref.ly/Isa40:1-Isa66:24) with its themes of: 

* salvation,
* forgiveness,
* praise,
* joy, and
* the nations.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 12:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 12:2 (id: 15853)

* God's promise to save includes:

    + reconciliation ([Isaiah 1:18](https://ref.ly/Isa1:18)),
        + participation ([Isaiah 11:10](https://ref.ly/Isa11:10)),
        + cleansing and protection ([Isaiah 4:3–6](https://ref.ly/Isa4:3-Isa4:6)),
        + peace and confidence ([Isaiah 32:16–17](https://ref.ly/Isa32:16-Isa32:17)), and
        + restoration ([Isaiah 11:6–9](https://ref.ly/Isa11:6-Isa11:9)).
* LORD GOD (Hebrew *Yah Yahweh*): The first word (*Yah*) is a shorter form of the second (*Yahweh*).
* *the LORD GOD is my strength and my song*: See [Exodus 15:2](https://ref.ly/Exod15:2); [Psalm 118:14](https://ref.ly/Ps118:14).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 12:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Isaiah 13:1 (id: 15865)

At the time of this prophecy, Assyria was the major power. Isaiah anticipated the rise of Babylon as an even crueler kingdom that would destroy Judah and Jerusalem ([Isaiah 39:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa39:6-Isa39:7); chapters [46–47](https://ref.ly/Isa46:1-Isa47:15)). 

The universal language in [Isaiah 13:1–14:23](https://ref.ly/Isa13:1-Isa14:23) suggests that Babylon symbolizes all wicked and arrogant nations, similar to "Babylon the Great" in the book of Revelation ([Revelation 18](https://ref.ly/Rev18:1-Rev18:24)). So, while this description of Babylon's fall refers to the historic fall in 539 BC, it also applies to the final judgment against the ultimate kingdom of evil ([Revelation 19](https://ref.ly/Rev19:1-Rev19:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 13:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Utterance (ID: `keyterm:Utterance`); Have a Vision (ID: `keyterm:HaveAVision`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Amoz (ID: `person:Amoz`)

## Isaiah 13:1–23.18 (id: 15857)

This section contains prophecies of judgment against the nations. By including a prophecy against Judah and Jerusalem ([Isaiah 22:1–25](https://ref.ly/Isa22:1-Isa22:25)) in the midst of prophecies against the pagan nations, Isaiah emphasized that Israel’s identity as God’s people would not protect them from God’s punishment when they sinned as the other nations do.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 13:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Utterance (ID: `keyterm:Utterance`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Nile River (ID: `place:Nile`); Tarshish (ID: `place:Tarshish`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`); Kir (ID: `place:Kir`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`); David (ID: `person:David`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Sidon (ID: `place:Sidon`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Heshbon (ID: `place:Heshbon`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`)

## Isaiah 13:2 (id: 15867)

* A signal flag or banner, visible from far away, was often placed on a bare hilltop as a call to battle.
* God opposes Babylon as the symbolic representative of all evil kingdoms.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 13:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Standard (ID: `realia:Standard`)

## Isaiah 13:5 (id: 15868)

* Just as Assyria was a weapon of God's wrath ([Isaiah 9:4](https://ref.ly/Isa9:4); [10:5](https://ref.ly/Isa10:5), [15](https://ref.ly/Isa10:15)), other nations act as the Lord’s weapons and carry out his will. The Medes and Persians conquered Babylon in 539 BC.
* God’s wrath shows his justice and his intent to restore order by destroying evildoers ([Isaiah 10:25–26](https://ref.ly/Isa10:25-Isa10:26); see [Lamentations 2:22](https://ref.ly/Lam2:22); [Ezekiel 7:19](https://ref.ly/Ezek7:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 13:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 13:6 (id: 15869)

When pronouncing judgment that is still in the future, the prophets sometimes spoke as though it had already arrived. This emphasized the urgency of turning back to God ([Ezekiel 30:3](https://ref.ly/Ezek30:3); [Joel 1:15](https://ref.ly/Joel1:15); [3:14](https://ref.ly/Joel3:14); [Obadiah 1:15](https://ref.ly/Obad1:15); [Zephaniah 1:7](https://ref.ly/Zeph1:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 13:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 13:8 (id: 15870)

*writhe like a woman in labor*: The day of the Lord brings sudden and overpowering pain and fear for the wicked (see also [Jeremiah 30:6](https://ref.ly/Jer30:6); [Hosea 13:13](https://ref.ly/Hos13:13); [Micah 4:9](https://ref.ly/Mic4:9), [10](https://ref.ly/Mic4:10); [5:3](https://ref.ly/Mic5:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 13:8


## Isaiah 13:10 (id: 15858)

Cosmic upheaval and darkness are common images for the day of the Lord, a time of God’s judgment (see also [Ezekiel 32:7–8](https://ref.ly/Ezek32:7-Ezek32:8); [Joel 2:10](https://ref.ly/Joel2:10), [31](https://ref.ly/Joel2:31); [3:15](https://ref.ly/Joel3:15); [Matthew 24:29](https://ref.ly/Matt24:29); [Mark 13:24](https://ref.ly/Mark13:24); [Revelation 6:12–13](https://ref.ly/Rev6:12-Rev6:13); [8:12](https://ref.ly/Rev8:12); compare [Isaiah 30:26](https://ref.ly/Isa30:26)).

## Isaiah 13:20 (id: 15866)

*She will never be inhabited … from generation to generation*: In Scripture, Babylon often represents idolatry (worship of false gods), immorality, imperial power, and oppression. It stands for human pride and rebellion against God.

One day, it will come to a complete end (see [Revelation 18:2](https://ref.ly/Rev18:2); [19:2](https://ref.ly/Rev19:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 13:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Arabians (ID: `group:Arabia`); Arabia (ID: `place:Arabia`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Isaiah 14:1 (id: 15879)

* The Hebrew word translated as *mercy* uses the image of a mother's care for her child to show God's merciful love for his people (see also [Isaiah 49:15](https://ref.ly/Isa49:15); [66:13](https://ref.ly/Isa66:13)).
* God chose Israel as his special people when he called Abraham (see [Isaiah 41:8](https://ref.ly/Isa41:8); [Genesis 12:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:3)). Although their status did not change during the exile in Babylon, they faced God's anger like any other wicked nation because they rejected him.
* *settle them in their own land*: This promise began to be fulfilled in 538 BC (see [Ezra 1:1–11](https://ref.ly/Ezra1:1-Ezra1:11)).
* People from many nations would join Israel as the people of God (see also [Isaiah 2:3–4](https://ref.ly/Isa2:3-Isa2:4); [11:11–12](https://ref.ly/Isa11:11-Isa11:12); [19:18–25](https://ref.ly/Isa19:18-Isa19:25); [60:1–14](https://ref.ly/Isa60:1-Isa60:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 14:1–2 (id: 15871)

This message for Israel separates two prophecies of judgment against Babylon ([Isaiah 13](https://ref.ly/Isa13:1-Isa13:22); [14:3–23](https://ref.ly/Isa14:3-Isa14:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 14:3 (id: 15891)

## Isaiah 14:3–23 (id: 15888)

This taunting song about the king of Babylon is written like a funeral song. It speaks as if the king has already died.

The song mocks him and announces his downfall (compare [Revelation 18](https://ref.ly/Rev18:1-Rev18:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:3-23

## Isaiah 14:4 (id: 15892)

A *song of contempt* is a mocking song. It makes fun of someone by comparing that person to something else.

In this case, the king of Babylon is compared to a dead man going down to the place of the dead. The song imagines him entering that realm in defeat.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Proverb (ID: `keyterm:Saying`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`)

## Isaiah 14:7–8 (id: 15893)

* The land and people will be peaceful and calm because the oppression has ended and the king of Babylon has died.
* All of creation will join in praise, able to sing again (see also [Isaiah 42:11](https://ref.ly/Isa42:11); [44:23](https://ref.ly/Isa44:23); compare [Romans 8:22](https://ref.ly/Rom8:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:7-8

## Isaiah 14:9 (id: 15894)

* The Babylonians viewed the place of the dead, called *Sheol* in Hebrew, as a place from which no one returns.
* *rise from their thrones*: The thrones represent the Babylonian belief that the afterlife continues like the present life. It seems the other kings are honoring Babylon's great king, but the following verses reveal a different story.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Throne (ID: `realia:Throne`)

## Isaiah 14:10 (id: 15872)

*weak, as we are*: The Babylonian king had no power over anyone after death and could not leave Sheol. The Israelites will mock this great king who seemed strong on earth but had no weaknesses.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:10


## Isaiah 14:11 (id: 15873)

* Babylon's strength, power, and music from the harp ended, and its greatness was destroyed (see also [Revelation 18:22](https://ref.ly/Rev18:22)).
* Maggots and worms represented death and decay ([Isaiah 66:24](https://ref.ly/Isa66:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Worm (ID: `fauna:Maggot`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Lyre (ID: `realia:Lyre`)

## Isaiah 14:12 (id: 15874)

* *fallen from heaven, O day star*: These words refer to a well\-known Canaanite story. In that story, the god Helel rebelled against the god El, who was the chief god of the Canaanite people. Helel was cast down from heaven.

    Isaiah uses this image to describe the fall of the king of Babylon. Some interpret the fall of the king of Babylon as symbolizing the fall of Satan (see [Ezekiel 28](https://ref.ly/Ezek28:1-Ezek28:26); [Luke 10:18](https://ref.ly/Luke10:18); [Revelation 12:9](https://ref.ly/Rev12:9)). However, there is little evidence that Isaiah meant this directly. He was speaking about the historical king of Babylon.

* *son of the dawn*: This refers to the morning star, which appears briefly at dawn and then disappears as the sun rises. In the Latin Vulgate, this term is translated *Lucifer*, which means “morning star.” In later Christian tradition, Lucifer became a name for Satan. However, the Hebrew text here does not clearly refer to Satan.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lucifer (ID: `deity:Lucifer`)

## Isaiah 14:13 (id: 15875)

This verse refers to a Canaanite belief about the gods. The Canaanites believed that their chief god, El, ruled from Mount Zaphon. This mountain was in the north (see [Psalm 48:2](https://ref.ly/Ps48:2); in the New Testament, similar language appears in [Matthew 11:23](https://ref.ly/Matt11:23); [Luke 10:15](https://ref.ly/Luke10:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zaphon (ID: `place:Zaphon`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Throne (ID: `realia:Throne`)

*Most High*: See [Genesis 14:19–22](https://ref.ly/Gen14:19-Gen14:22).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 14:15–17 (id: 15877)

This is a restatement of [14:9–11](https://ref.ly/Isa14:9-Isa14:11). The spirits in the place of the dead will be surprised and amazed that the Babylonian king, who ruled the world with his merciless might, has absolutely no power in death.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:15-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)

## Isaiah 14:18–20 (id: 15878)

The absence of a proper burial was a sign of great shame and dishonor. Unlike other kings, the king of Babylon would be disgraced in judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:18-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`)

## Isaiah 14:21 (id: 15880)

As another sign of disgrace, the king of Babylon would have no children to continue his legacy. Because of their father's sins, God considers individuals in relation to their families and people. Here, the Babylonian king's children shared responsibility for their father's actions (see also [Deuteronomy 5:9–10](https://ref.ly/Deut5:9-Deut5:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 14:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`)

* The hilltops were sites for pagan shrines (see [Isaiah 15:2](https://ref.ly/Isa15:2)).
* No one will be able to save them, including the idols the Moabites called on for protection (see [Isaiah 40:18–19](https://ref.ly/Isa40:18-Isa40:19); [44:18–19](https://ref.ly/Isa44:18-Isa44:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 16:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moab (ID: `place:Moab`); Stone Pine (ID: `flora:Pine`); High Place (ID: `realia:HighPlace`)

## Isaiah 16:14 (id: 15905)

* *In three years, as a hired worker counts the years* (see also [Isaiah 21:16](https://ref.ly/Isa21:16)): A man who sold himself into servitude would calculate the remaining time down to the last day. This verse refers to unknown events.
* The glory of Moab will be ended*,* probably because of the Assyrian conquest of Moab (late 700s BC).
* *those who are left will be few and feeble*: Though reduced by war, the Moabites still existed during Nehemiah’s time ([Nehemiah 13:23](https://ref.ly/Neh13:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 16:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Moab (ID: `place:Moab`)

## Isaiah 17:1 (id: 15919)

Damascus, the leader of the alliance between Syria and Israel against Judah, fell when the Assyrian king Tiglath\-pileser III invaded in 732 BC.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 17:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Utterance (ID: `keyterm:Utterance`); Damascus (ID: `place:Damascus`)

## Isaiah 17:1–20.6 (id: 15913)

This section includes predictions of judgment against Damascus, Israel, Ethiopia, and Egypt. The scene shifts from the crisis in 734 BC (the alliance of Syria and Israel; see study note on Isaiah 7:1–25) to the Assyrian crisis in 701 BC (see [Isaiah 36–37](https://ref.ly/Isa36:1-Isa37:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 17:1-20:6

## Isaiah 17:2 (id: 15920)

* The towns of Aroer were east of the Dead Sea (see [2 Kings 10:32–33](https://ref.ly/2Kgs10:32-2Kgs10:33)).
* *they will be left to the flocks*: The city would serve no other purpose.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 17:2

## Isaiah 17:3 (id: 15921)

The strategic fortified towns of Israel were the pride of Israel, but they could not resist God’s purposes.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 17:3

## Isaiah 17:4 (id: 15923)

* *the splendor of Jacob will fade*: God will judge all human pride.
* Israel, once strong, will become weak. The nation will waste away like a sick person.

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Isaiah 17:4–11 (id: 15922)

The vision shifts from Damascus ([Isaiah 17:1–3](https://ref.ly/Isa17:1-Isa17:3)) to Israel's northern kingdom. Damascus and Israel were allies against Judah, but Assyria later destroyed both.

The Valley of Rephaim was a fertile area in Judah. However, it appeared barren after each harvest.

Although the God of Israel judged the wickedness of Israel, he was still its God.

## Isaiah 17:7 (id: 15926)

* The people mentioned here are either Israelites or all of humanity.
* *look … turn their eyes*: They will once more trust in the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 17:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 17:8 (id: 15927)

Canaanites and then Israelites worshiped the fertility goddess Asherah in wooded areas or, in the absence of wooded areas, by using Asherah poles (see [Isaiah 27:9](https://ref.ly/Isa27:9); [65:3](https://ref.ly/Isa65:3); [Micah 5:14](https://ref.ly/Mic5:14)).

## Isaiah 17:9 (id: 15928)

The Hivites and Amorites were part of the original people living in Canaan. The Israelites were to destroy them when they conquered the land (see [Deuteronomy 20:17](https://ref.ly/Deut20:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 17:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`)

## Isaiah 17:10 (id: 15914)

* The Israelites combined their faith in the Lord with Baal worship. They turned away from the Lord, who is the only God and will not share devotion with idols.
* The term *Rock* refers to the Lord's ability to provide refuge (see [Isaiah 26:4](https://ref.ly/Isa26:4); [30:29](https://ref.ly/Isa30:29); [Psalm 18:2](https://ref.ly/Ps18:2), [46](https://ref.ly/Ps18:46)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 17:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`)

## Isaiah 17:12 (id: 15916)

At times, the sea represents opposition to God (see [Job 7:12](https://ref.ly/Job7:12); [26:12](https://ref.ly/Job26:12); [Psalms 46:3](https://ref.ly/Ps46:3); [93:3](https://ref.ly/Ps93:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 17:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`)

## Isaiah 17:12–14 (id: 15915)

God was present with Judah during the crisis.

## Isaiah 17:13 (id: 15917)

*He rebukes them*: The Lord rules over the nations, just as he rules over the sea ([Psalm 65:7](https://ref.ly/Ps65:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 17:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`)

## Isaiah 17:14 (id: 15918)

*In the evening … Before morning*: Trouble may come at night, but it will not last long.

Even in frightening situations, Israel can trust the Lord. He is able to rescue his people quickly (see [Isaiah 37:36–38](https://ref.ly/Isa37:36-Isa37:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 17:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lot (ID: `keyterm:Lot`)

## Isaiah 18:1 (id: 15929)

Ethiopia was located to the south of Egypt. It marked the southern border of Egypt’s kingdom.

At that time, Egypt was ruled by an Ethiopian dynasty (a family line of kings). One of its rulers was Piankhi, also known as Piye.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 18:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`); Locust (ID: `fauna:Locust`)

## Isaiah 18:3 (id: 15930)

Both the banner and ram's horn were used to organize and send signals to armies during battles (see [Judges 3:27](https://ref.ly/Judg3:27); [6:34](https://ref.ly/Judg6:34); [2 Samuel 2:28](https://ref.ly/2Sam2:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 18:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Standard (ID: `realia:Standard`); Rams Horn (ID: `realia:RamsHorn`)

## Isaiah 18:4 (id: 15931)

The Lord is untroubled by human activities. He watches quietly from his dwelling place and acts when he chooses (see [Psalm 2:1–4](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 18:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 18:5 (id: 15932)

*the flower becomes a ripening grape*: Ethiopia had made every effort to form alliances to protect itself against Assyria, but its alliance with Judah would not help.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 18:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pruning Knife (ID: `realia:PruningKnife`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Isaiah 18:7 (id: 15933)

Conquered or weaker nations often had to pay tribute to a dominant king in the region (see [Isiah 16:1](https://ref.ly/Isa16:1); [2 Kings 3:4–5](https://ref.ly/2Kgs3:4-2Kgs3:5)). The Ethiopians brought gifts to honor the Lord for defeating the Assyrians (see also [2 Chronicles 32:23](https://ref.ly/2Chr32:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 18:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

* Riding on a swift cloud shows God's control and power over creation ([Psalm 104:3](https://ref.ly/Ps104:3)). He is coming to judge Judah's enemies (see [Deuteronomy 33:26](https://ref.ly/Deut33:26); [Psalms 18:9–10](https://ref.ly/Ps18:9-Ps18:10); [68:4](https://ref.ly/Ps68:4); [97:2–3](https://ref.ly/Ps97:2-Ps97:3)).
* The idols of Egypt tremble because they are weak and powerless, unable to help (see [Isaiah 40:18](https://ref.ly/Isa40:18); [45:16](https://ref.ly/Isa45:16); [46:1](https://ref.ly/Isa46:1)).

## Isaiah 19:2 (id: 15951)

Egypt experienced many civil wars that damaged the country. The nation's fragile unity was falling apart.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Isaiah 19:3 (id: 15952)

People called upon idols, spirits, mediums, and those who consult the spirits of the dead to try to prevent destruction (see [Isaiah 8:19–22](https://ref.ly/Isa8:19-Isa8:22); [47:12–13](https://ref.ly/Isa47:12-Isa47:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:3

## Isaiah 19:4 (id: 15953)

*harsh master*: In 671 BC, the Assyrian king Esarhaddon invaded Egypt and destroyed Memphis, the royal city. In 663 BC, Ashurbanipal attacked and destroyed Thebes (see [Nahum 3:8–10](https://ref.ly/Nah3:8-Nah3:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 19:5 (id: 15954)

The Nile River was essential for Egypt's survival. Any change in its flooding pattern impacted the entire country. In ancient Egypt, people saw the Nile as a god. However, the true God could easily dry it up.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:5


## Isaiah 19:9 (id: 15955)

*The workers in flax … and the weavers of fine linen will turn pale*: The drought destroyed the flax crops. As a result, the linen industry could not continue.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cotton (ID: `flora:Cotton`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Flax (ID: `flora:Flax`)

## Isaiah 19:11 (id: 15937)

* Zoan was the capital of Egypt, located in the Nile delta (see [Numbers 13:22](https://ref.ly/Num13:22)).
* *princes … wise counselors*: The king relied on the strategies and advice of wise men ([Isaiah 19:12](https://ref.ly/Isa19:12); see [Genesis 41:39–40](https://ref.ly/Gen41:39-Gen41:40)).
* The officials of Zoan were famous for their wise ancestors, and Egypt often boasted about them.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zoan (ID: `place:Zoan`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.8`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Fool (ID: `keyterm:Fool`)

## Isaiah 19:11–15 (id: 15936)

The prophet mocked the leaders and wise men of Egypt, who could not prevent the disaster.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:11-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Zoan (ID: `place:Zoan`); Fool (ID: `keyterm:Fool`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.8`); Memphis (ID: `place:Memphis`); Palm Branch and Reed (ID: `keyterm:PalmBranchAndReed`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Date Palm (ID: `flora:DatePalm`)

## Isaiah 19:12 (id: 15938)

*Where are your wise men now?*: Compare [1 Corinthians 1:20](https://ref.ly/1Cor1:20).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 19:13 (id: 15939)

Memphis was an ancient capital in Egypt located south of the Nile Delta. It was destroyed by Esarhaddon in 671 BC.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Zoan (ID: `place:Zoan`); Memphis (ID: `place:Memphis`)

## Isaiah 19:15 (id: 15940)

* *There is nothing Egypt can do*: No leader, idol, or human wisdom can stop God’s judgment.
* The head and the tail represented all of Egypt's leaders, from the most important to the least important (see [Isaiah 9:14–15](https://ref.ly/Isa9:14-Isa9:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Palm Branch and Reed (ID: `keyterm:PalmBranchAndReed`); Date Palm (ID: `flora:DatePalm`)

## Isaiah 19:16 (id: 15941)

*The uplifted hand* (literally *waving the hand*) was a threatening gesture.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 19:18 (id: 15942)

* *five cities in the land of Egypt*: This likely means "a number of cities" (see also [Isaiah 17:6](https://ref.ly/Isa17:6); [30:17](https://ref.ly/Isa30:17)).
* Jews living in Egypt after Jerusalem's destruction spoke Hebrew, referred to here as the language of Canaan.
* Heliopolis was dedicated to Re, the sun god. When Judah resisted the Lord, Isaiah imagined the Egyptians following the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** City of Destruction (ID: `place:CityOfDestruction`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Isaiah 19:19 (id: 15944)

This altar or monument might refer to the temple built by Jews at Elephantine in Egypt around 500 BC. It could also refer to another Jewish place of worship or a memorial the Egyptians made to honor the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Isaiah 19:19–25 (id: 15943)

The new Egypt would receive a place in God’s purposes together with Assyria and Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:19-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Vow (ID: `keyterm:Vow`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Assyrians (ID: `group:Assyria`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Isaiah 19:20 (id: 15946)

* The altar ([Isaiah 19:19](https://ref.ly/Isa19:19)) would be a sign and a witness of the Egyptians’ religious connection with Israel (see [19:24–25](https://ref.ly/Isa19:24-Isa19:25)).
* God answers the prayers of those who ask the Lord for help.
* The Lord is a savior; he would protect the Egyptians just as he protected Jerusalem from the Assyrians.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 19:22 (id: 15947)

* *strike Egypt*: The Hebrew verb here is the same as the one used for the tenth plague ([Exodus 12:13](https://ref.ly/Exod12:13), [23](https://ref.ly/Exod12:23), [27](https://ref.ly/Exod12:27)), reminding us of God's actions during the exodus.
* The Lord, who sends a plague, can also heal.
* *He will hear their prayers and heal them*: This expresses a covenantal relationship between the Lord and Egypt (see [Isaiah 65:24](https://ref.ly/Isa65:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 19:23 (id: 15948)

* Traditionally, Egypt and Assyria were enemies of each other and of Israel. They were deeply involved in idol worship, but they would abandon their hostility and idols to worship the true God.
* In the rugged terrain of the ancient Near East, a highway allowed for safe and easy travel and trade (see [Isaiah 11:16](https://ref.ly/Isa11:16); [35:8](https://ref.ly/Isa35:8); [40:3](https://ref.ly/Isa40:3); [62:10](https://ref.ly/Isa62:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Assyrians (ID: `group:Assyria`)

## Isaiah 19:24 (id: 15949)

Israel will be a blessing, fulfilling God's promise to Abraham ([Genesis 12:3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`)

## Isaiah 19:25 (id: 15950)

Two titles usually meant for Israel, *My people* and *My handiwork* (see [Isaiah 29:22](https://ref.ly/Isa29:22); [32:18](https://ref.ly/Isa32:18)), are used here for foreign nations. The Lord promised to consider those from other nations who turn to him as his special possession ([Isaiah 63:17](https://ref.ly/Isa63:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 19:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`)

## Isaiah 20:1 (id: 15975)

* Sargon of Assyria is Sargon II, who ruled from 721 to 705 BC.
* Ashdod was one of the five Philistine cities that rebelled against Assyria in 713 BC.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 20:1

## Isaiah 20:1–5 (id: 15974)

Ashdod’s reliance on Egypt and Ethiopia was useless. As a complement to his words, Isaiah walked about exposed and barefoot, a symbolic action for what God would do.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 20:1-5

## Isaiah 20:2 (id: 15976)

* Prophets often wore sackcloth or other rough clothing to show mourning for the people's sins (see [2 Kings 1:8](https://ref.ly/2Kgs1:8); [Mark 1:6](https://ref.ly/Mark1:6)).
* *naked*: This means exposed in some way, possibly even completely nude.

This symbolic event happened about three years before Ashdod fell (see [Isaiah 20:2](https://ref.ly/Isa20:2)). Through this event, Isaiah served as an example for the people (see [Isaiah 8:18](https://ref.ly/Isa8:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 20:3-4

## Isaiah 21:1 (id: 15989)

* *This is the burden against the Desert by the Sea*: This description of Babylon might ironically suggest that the land was physically lush but spiritually empty.
* *from the desert, from a land of terror*: (compare [Deuteronomy 8:15](https://ref.ly/Deut8:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 21:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Utterance (ID: `keyterm:Utterance`)

## Isaiah 21:2 (id: 15990)

* *A dire vision is declared to me*: The prophet makes Babylon's experience personal, which strengthens his poetry. The concise language and repeated sounds, like *The traitor still betrays* (Hebrew *habboged boged*) and *the destroyer still destroys* (Hebrew *hashoded shoded*; also in [Isaiah 33:1](https://ref.ly/Isa33:1)), add to this effect.
* The Elamites lived northeast of Babylon. They, along with the Medes, were major rivals of the Babylonians.
* *I will put an end to all her groaning*: Babylon caused suffering by attacking and enslaving many nations. The Elamites and Medes likely helped overthrow Babylon in 539 BC ([Isaiah 21:9](https://ref.ly/Isa21:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 21:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Elam (ID: `place:Elam`); Media (ID: `place:Media`)

## Isaiah 21:3 (id: 15991)

*I am bewildered to hear, I am dismayed to see*: Through his vision, Isaiah felt the fear of the Babylonian people (see also [Daniel 8:27](https://ref.ly/Dan8:27); [10:16–17](https://ref.ly/Dan10:16-Dan10:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 21:3


## Isaiah 21:7 (id: 15992)

The riders were messengers who reported what had taken place on the battlefield.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 21:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`)

## Isaiah 21:9 (id: 15993)

* *fallen is Babylon!*: Assyrian King Sennacherib destroyed Babylon in 689 BC. The final fall of Babylon occurred in 539 BC.

    In Scripture, Babylon symbolizes all ungodly power structures, including nations and kingdoms that do not follow God and his word. The apostle John offered hope with his prophecy that all Babylons will fall ([Revelation 14:8](https://ref.ly/Rev14:8); [18:2](https://ref.ly/Rev18:2)).

* *All the images of her gods lie shattered on the ground!*: False gods could not save the Babylonians (see [Isaiah 46:1–2](https://ref.ly/Isa46:1-Isa46:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 21:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`)

## Isaiah 21:10 (id: 15979)

* *O my people*: The focus returned to the people of Judah.
* The Babylonians would oppress (thresh and winnow) Judah, but Babylon would also fall.
* *I tell you what I have heard*: The prophet faithfully reported what the Lord revealed to him.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 21:10
