## Isaiah 22:1 (id: 16003)

*This is the burden against the Valley of Vision*: The meaning of the Hebrew phrase is unclear. If it refers to Jerusalem (see [Isaiah 22:5](https://ref.ly/Isa22:5)), it is ironic. Jerusalem is on a hill, and people did not usually think visions came from a *valley.* Similarly, Jerusalem was proud of its supposed religious *vision*, but its people were spiritually blind (see [Isaiah 6:10](https://ref.ly/Isa6:10); [42:18](https://ref.ly/Isa42:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 22:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Utterance (ID: `keyterm:Utterance`)

## Isaiah 22:1–4 (id: 15995)

The prophet scolds the people for celebrating the Assyrian retreat in 701 BC. They did not predict that the Babylonian army would later, in 586 BC, finish what the Assyrians could not: the destruction of Jerusalem.

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Utterance (ID: `keyterm:Utterance`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`)

## Isaiah 22:2 (id: 16007)

## Isaiah 22:8–11 (id: 16014)

The people of Jerusalem prepared for war. They counted their weapons, inspected the city walls, and made sure they had enough water.

However, they did not think about God’s plans (see [Isaiah 13:1–14:23](https://ref.ly/Isa13:1-Isa14:23)). They focused on their own efforts instead of seeking the Lord.

King Hezekiah played an important role in these preparations. However, the verbs in this passage are plural. Isaiah was speaking to the people as a group, not only to Hezekiah. This is different from the way Isaiah addressed Hezekiah’s father, Ahaz ([Isaiah 7:1–4](https://ref.ly/Isa7:1-Isa7:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 22:8-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** House (ID: `realia:House`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); House of the Forest of Lebanon (ID: `place:HouseOfTheForestOfLebanon`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); David (ID: `person:David`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Weapons (ID: `realia:Weapons`); Screen (ID: `realia:Screen`)

* Shebna's actions show that the people do not trust God to save Jerusalem from the Assyrian attack.
* A palace administrator had the top position in the royal court (see [Isaiah 22:21–22](https://ref.ly/Isa22:21-Isa22:22)).

## Isaiah 22:15–25 (id: 15998)

Shebna and Eliakim were two royal officials in King Hezekiah’s court (see [2 Kings 18:18](https://ref.ly/2Kgs18:18); [19:2](https://ref.ly/2Kgs19:2)). Shebna believed the Assyrians would surely kill him. So he planned for a well\-decorated burial place ([Isaiah 22:16](https://ref.ly/Isa22:16)). 

Although the rebuke of Shebna happened before Assyria’s siege of Jerusalem in 701 BC (see [Isaiah 22:19–21](https://ref.ly/Isa22:19-Isa22:21); [36:3](https://ref.ly/Isa36:3)), it relates well to the warnings in [22:1–13](https://ref.ly/Isa22:1-Isa22:13) about the later Babylonian siege.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 22:15-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Well-Established (ID: `keyterm:BeFirm`); Peg (ID: `realia:Peg.2`); Peg (ID: `realia:Peg`); Eliakim (ID: `person:Eliakim.3`); Hilkiah (ID: `person:Hilkiah`); Shebna (ID: `person:Shebna`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); David (ID: `person:David`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Sash (ID: `realia:Sash`); Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`); Clay Jar (ID: `realia:ClayJar`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`)

## Isaiah 22:16 (id: 16000)

Shebna was building an impressive tomb for himself. It was meant to be a monument. This large and costly burial place was designed to last for many generations.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 22:16

* *sling you into a wide land*: Shebna would not find a resting place or have a memorial (compare [Isaiah 14:11–20](https://ref.ly/Isa14:11-Isa14:20)).
* *your glorious chariots*: In his high position, Shebna lived in luxury.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 22:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`)

## Isaiah 22:19–20 (id: 16002)

*remove you from office*: Shebna ([Isaiah 22:15](https://ref.ly/Isa22:15)) was demoted to court secretary by the time of Jerusalem’s siege. Eliakim ([Isaiah 22:20](https://ref.ly/Isa22:20)) was the palace administrator ([36:3](https://ref.ly/Isa36:3)). Eliakim was a true servant of the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 22:19-20

## Isaiah 23:1 (id: 16024)

* The ships of Tarshish traveled the sea between the Phoenician coast and the port of Tarshish in Spain (see [Isaiah 2:16](https://ref.ly/Isa2:16); [23:6](https://ref.ly/Isa23:6)).
* *Word has reached them from the land of Cyprus*: Cyprus was the last stop for sailors returning from Spain before their final journey to Tyre.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 23:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`); Tarshish (ID: `place:Tarshish`); Cyprus (ID: `place:Cyprus`); Utterance (ID: `keyterm:Utterance`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Isaiah 23:1–18 (id: 16017)

Tyre was a wealthy seaport northwest of Israel. The connection between Judah and Tyre dates back to the time of David and Solomon ([1 Kings 5:8–9](https://ref.ly/1Kgs5:8-1Kgs5:9)). 

Tyre was near large forests and the Mediterranean Sea. It was a major trade center in the ancient Near East. Its ships were famous, and its colonies gave it great prestige and wealth. 

The city faced repeated attacks by the Assyrians, Babylonians, and Macedonians, and Alexander the Great conquered it in 332 BC. Isaiah not only predicted Tyre's downfall but also warned those whose wealth and security lead to pride instead of reliance on God (see [Isaiah 2:12–16](https://ref.ly/Isa2:12-Isa2:16); [Matthew 11:21](https://ref.ly/Matt11:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 23:1-18

## Isaiah 23:2 (id: 16025)

* Sidon was a Phoenician port located to the north of Tyre.
* Tyre’s commercial network of traders was extensive.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 23:2

## Isaiah 23:3 (id: 16026)

* People harvested fish, crops, flax, and linen along the Nile (see [Isaiah 19](https://ref.ly/Isa19:1-Isa19:25)).
* Tyre was a global marketplace, similar to modern cities like New York, Hong Kong, or London.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 23:3

## Isaiah 23:4 (id: 16027)

* Part of the great port city of Tyre was on an island. The island city was a sea fortress, but it fell in 332 BC.
* *I have not been in labor or given birth. I have not raised young men or brought up young women*: This metaphor was a judgment on Tyre’s colonial ports around the Mediterranean Sea. In the ancient world, reaching old age without children was considered a terrible fate (see [Isaiah 49:21](https://ref.ly/Isa49:21); [54:1](https://ref.ly/Isa54:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 23:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sidon (ID: `place:Sidon`); Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`)

## Isaiah 23:8 (id: 16029)

* The Lord had brought this disaster on Tyre ([Isaiah 23:9](https://ref.ly/Isa23:9)).

## Isaiah 23:10 (id: 16018)

*Cultivate your land like the Nile*: This difficult phrase means either: 

1. that the sailors returning from Tarshish were invited to loot the ruined Tyre; or
2. that the people of Tarshish had to return to farming to earn a living, since Tyre was destroyed and trading was no longer possible.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 23:10

## Isaiah 23:18 (id: 16023)

* Although Tyre earned money through dishonest trade, the profits will go to the Lord instead of being wasted.

* *lays waste the earth and leaves it in ruins*: There is a play here on the Hebrew words *baqaq* (*lays waste*) and *balaq* (*leaves it in ruins*). The devastation will start with Judah and spread to the entire world (see [1 Peter 4:17](https://ref.ly/1Pet4:17)).
* All the earth will face destruction, including its people and political systems.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 24:1

## Isaiah 24:1–23 (id: 16030)

## Isaiah 24:1–27.13 (id: 16031)

This section is often called the *Little Apocalypse*. It is given this name because it has similarities to the book of Revelation.

In these chapters, Isaiah moves from his present time into a vision of the future. The language is broad and universal. Because of this, it is difficult to connect the events to one specific historical situation.

These chapters are not meant to provide a detailed timeline of future events. They are not a historical blueprint. Instead, Isaiah uses vivid images to describe a dramatic future world that is both similar to and different from the present.

The vision includes elements of both the old age and the new age. For example, the people of Zion are described as righteous (new), yet they still wait for their full redemption (old). This pattern fits with the New Testament teaching that the future age has already begun but has not yet fully arrived ([2 Corinthians 4:4](https://ref.ly/2Cor4:4); [Galatians 1:4](https://ref.ly/Gal1:4)). 

Peter wrote that believers were already living in the last days ([1 Peter 1:12](https://ref.ly/1Pet1:12); [2 Peter 3:3](https://ref.ly/2Pet3:3)). At the same time, he said that the final day of the Lord is still future ([2 Peter 3:10](https://ref.ly/2Pet3:10)). These passages show that the present age and the future age overlap.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 24:1-27:13

## Isaiah 24:4 (id: 16042)

*earth … withers; the world languishes and fades*: The prophets often used drought imagery to capture the attention of those who depend on the land (see also [Jeremiah 23:10](https://ref.ly/Jer23:10); [Joel 1:12](https://ref.ly/Joel1:12); [Amos 1:2](https://ref.ly/Amos1:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 24:4


## Isaiah 24:4–13 (id: 16041)

All humanity is guilty ([Romans 3:23](https://ref.ly/Rom3:23)) and faces condemnation (see [Isaiah 59:1–15](https://ref.ly/Isa59:1-Isa59:15)). Judgment is like a failed grape harvest that stops drinking and feasting.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 24:4-13

## Isaiah 24:5 (id: 16043)

Isaiah likely referred to the everlasting covenant that God made with all humanity through Noah ([Genesis 9:8–17](https://ref.ly/Gen9:8-Gen9:17)). The prophet noticed a similarity between the wickedness of his time and that of the world before the flood.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 24:5

## Isaiah 24:6 (id: 16044)

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 24:6

## Isaiah 24:16 (id: 16036)

* *I am wasting away*: The prophets knew the emotional pain caused by their people's sins and its consequences (see [Isaiah 15:5](https://ref.ly/Isa15:5); [21:4](https://ref.ly/Isa21:4); [Jeremiah 15:10](https://ref.ly/Jer15:10); [Micah 1:8](https://ref.ly/Mic1:8)).

## Isaiah 24:18 (id: 16037)

* No one can avoid the day of the Lord (see study note on Isaiah 2:5–22).
* *the windows of heaven are open*: This image is similar to the great flood in Noah's time (see study note on Genesis 7:11–12).
* *the foundations of the earth are shaken*: An earthquake often marks a *theophany*, which is a physical sign of God's presence (see also [Isaiah 6:4](https://ref.ly/Isa6:4); [13:13](https://ref.ly/Isa13:13); [19:1](https://ref.ly/Isa19:1)).

## Isaiah 24:21 (id: 16039)

The phrase translated as *host of heaven* comes from the Hebrew *tseba’ hammarom*, which means “armed army of the heavens.” This phrase may refer to pagan gods that people believed were represented by the stars.

It may also refer to angels. Some scholars think it could even include demons ([Revelation 12:4](https://ref.ly/Rev12:4), [9](https://ref.ly/Rev12:9)). The judgment described here will reach every part of creation. It will affect both the earth and the heavens ([Isaiah 13:13](https://ref.ly/Isa13:13); [34:5](https://ref.ly/Isa34:5); see [Ephesians 6:11–12](https://ref.ly/Eph6:11-Eph6:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 24:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 26:1 (id: 16065)

* The *city* refers to Zion, the eternal city of God, where the Lord is present to protect and bless his people (see [Isaiah 1:8](https://ref.ly/Isa1:8), [27](https://ref.ly/Isa1:27); see also [Psalm 46](https://ref.ly/Ps46:1-Ps46:11)). Zion stands in direct contrast with the "fortified towns" of the nations ([Isaiah 25:2](https://ref.ly/Isa25:2); [26:5](https://ref.ly/Isa26:5)), which are helpless and reduced to "a heap of rubble."
* The walls of God's salvation protectively enclose his people. No one can harm them again.

* *we wait for You* (see [Isaiah 26:3–4](https://ref.ly/Isa26:3-Isa26:4)): Waiting means trusting in the Lord and hoping for his redemption.
* People demonstrate their commitment to God by obeying his laws; just saying it is not enough.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 26:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 26:9 (id: 16075)

*longs for You … seeks You*: Compare [Psalm 63:1](https://ref.ly/Ps63:1).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 26:9


## Isaiah 26:10 (id: 16058)

God has shown kindness to those who do wrong. Even though they deserve judgment (see also [Romans 2:4](https://ref.ly/Rom2:4); [2 Peter 3:9](https://ref.ly/2Pet3:9)), he has been patient and forgiving and has blessed them. However, one day, time will run out.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 26:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 26:11 (id: 16059)

* A raised fist shows anger and a threat of judgment (see also [Isaiah 19:16](https://ref.ly/Isa19:16)).
* Fire was often an image for God’s judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 26:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`)

## Isaiah 26:15 (id: 16060)

This verse is a confident expression of thanks for God’s blessings. In Isaiah’s time, during King Hezekiah’s prosperous reign, the borders of the nation of Israel were extended*,* and people gave glory to God for his great deeds.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 26:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bring Honor and Glory on Oneself (ID: `keyterm:BringHonorAndGloryOnOneself`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 26:16 (id: 16061)

While waiting for God's salvation ([Isaiah 26:1](https://ref.ly/Isa26:1)), the faithful were in distress and longed for justice. They prayed under the weight of God's discipline. During Hezekiah's time, Judah was in distress when Sennacherib attacked and destroyed the entire nation except for Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 26:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 26:17 (id: 16062)

The image of a pregnant woman in childbirth shows the nation's pain when Assyrian King Sennacherib attacked in 701 BC (see [Isaiah 37:3](https://ref.ly/Isa37:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 26:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 26:18 (id: 16063)

Only God can give salvation and bring life (see [Isaiah 59:9–15](https://ref.ly/Isa59:9-Isa59:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 26:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Isaiah 26:19 (id: 16064)

*Your dead will liv**e*: There is hope for the remnant. Some hold the minimal interpretation that this means the people would be restored to the land after the exile in Babylon (see [Ezekiel 37:11–12](https://ref.ly/Ezek37:11-Ezek37:12)) or that the Lord would preserve Jerusalem (see [Isaiah 29:4](https://ref.ly/Isa29:4), [6–8](https://ref.ly/Isa29:6-Isa29:8)). 

More likely, it refers to the resurrection of the body from the grave (see [Isaiah 25:7–8](https://ref.ly/Isa25:7-Isa25:8)). The Old Testament says little about the resurrection, but this text anticipates more detailed discussion in the New Testament (see [1 Corinthians 15:12–58](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:12-1Cor15:58)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 26:19


## Isaiah 26:20 (id: 16066)

God will punish the wicked, so the faithful should go home and avoid trouble.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 26:20


## Isaiah 26:21 (id: 16067)

The wicked hold power now, but their oppressive rule will end on the day of the Lord's wrath. On that day, he will punish them for their sins.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 26:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`)

## Isaiah 27:1 (id: 16080)

Israel used the image of Leviathan to represent evil powers that oppose God. Leviathan's death symbolizes the end of evil, Satan, demons, and forces hostile to God (see [Isaiah 51:9](https://ref.ly/Isa51:9); [Psalms 74:14](https://ref.ly/Ps74:14); [104:7–9](https://ref.ly/Ps104:7-Ps104:9), [26](https://ref.ly/Ps104:26); see also the serpent in [Genesis 3](https://ref.ly/Gen3:1-Gen3:24); [Revelation 12](https://ref.ly/Rev12:1-Rev12:18); [13](https://ref.ly/Rev13:1-Rev13:18); [16:13](https://ref.ly/Rev16:13); [20:2](https://ref.ly/Rev20:2), [10](https://ref.ly/Rev20:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Leviathan (ID: `fauna:GiantCroccodile`); Dragon (ID: `fauna:Dragon`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`)

## Isaiah 27:2–6 (id: 16081)

In the Song of the Fruitful Vineyard, the vineyard represents God’s people (compare [Isaiah 5:1–7](https://ref.ly/Isa5:1-Isa5:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:2-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`)

## Isaiah 27:3 (id: 16082)

* God calls himself the Lord of the covenant to assure his people that his words and actions are trustworthy (see [Exodus 6:2–3](https://ref.ly/Exod6:2-Exod6:3)). This phrase appears many times in Isaiah.
* *keeper … water … guard*: The Lord will give even greater care and protection to his ultimate vineyard than he did to Israel, his first vineyard (see [Isaiah 5:1–2](https://ref.ly/Isa5:1-Isa5:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 27:5 (id: 16083)

* God wants everyone to turn to him and trust him for help.
* God offers his people a chance to make peace with him.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`)

## Isaiah 27:6 (id: 16084)

* Jacob’s descendants will grow, and the remaining people will increase ([Isaiah 37:31](https://ref.ly/Isa37:31); see also [5:24](https://ref.ly/Isa5:24); [14:30](https://ref.ly/Isa14:30); for application to the Messiah, see [11:1](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1), [10](https://ref.ly/Isa11:10)).
* *fill the whole world*: This prophecy goes beyond Israel's return from exile to the second coming of the Lord Jesus. It includes all righteous children of God (Jews and non\-Jews) who suffer but stay obedient while waiting for complete redemption (see [Isaiah 26:18](https://ref.ly/Isa26:18); [John 15:1–8](https://ref.ly/John15:1-John15:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Isaiah 27:7 (id: 16086)

Even though Israel was God's chosen people, they faced punishment like other wicked nations. However, Israel was not entirely destroyed. A small remnant survived.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:7


## Isaiah 27:7–11 (id: 16085)

Isaiah reminded readers of the reasons for the exile and judgment: 

* Israel’s sinfulness,
* God’s righteous judgment, and
* the absence of divine compassion.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:7-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Asherah (ID: `deity:Asherah`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Sacred Pole (ID: `realia:SacredPole`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Isaiah 27:8 (id: 16087)

* The Assyrians forced many people from the northern kingdom of Israel into exile when Samaria fell in 722 BC. The Babylonians exiled many from Judah between 605 and 586 BC.
* Storms from the east were famous for their destructive effects (see [Job 27:21](https://ref.ly/Job27:21); [38:24](https://ref.ly/Job38:24); [Jeremiah 4:11](https://ref.ly/Jer4:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:8


## Isaiah 27:9 (id: 16088)

* God's justice requires him to punish sin to correct behavior and purify. The exile in Babylon happened to cleanse Israel's wickedness and remove all of Israel's sins (see [Romans 11:27](https://ref.ly/Rom11:27)).
* This aimed to end all pagan worship in Israel, including the incense altars and the poles used for worshiping the goddess Asherah.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Asherah (ID: `deity:Asherah`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Sacred Pole (ID: `realia:SacredPole`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`)

## Isaiah 27:10 (id: 16076)

Places previously associated with human power and accomplishment would be ruined to the extent that animals would graze in them.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Isaiah 27:11 (id: 16077)

The people were living outside God’s covenant care (his special promise relationship with them). They had turned away from him.

This was a foolish choice. They rejected the protection and blessing that came from belonging to God.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`)

## Isaiah 27:12 (id: 16078)

* God's withholding of mercy from Israel ([Isaiah 27:11](https://ref.ly/Isa27:11)) will be temporary.
* *from the flowing Euphrates to the Wadi of Egypt*: God's remnant will return from Assyria and Egypt to the land God promised to Abraham ([Genesis 15:18](https://ref.ly/Gen15:18); see [1 Kings 4:21](https://ref.ly/1Kgs4:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:12

## Isaiah 27:13 (id: 16079)

The great ram’s horn was blown to gather the people. Its loud sound called them together (see [Matthew 24:31](https://ref.ly/Matt24:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 27:13

## Isaiah 28:1–33.24 (id: 16090)

This section is connected by a series of six threats of judgment or woes (compare [Isaiah 5:8–23](https://ref.ly/Isa5:8-Isa5:23)). After the initial address to Samaria in [Isaiah 28:1–13](https://ref.ly/Isa28:1-Isa28:13), the rest of the section addresses Judah. This is mainly because Judah tends to trust Egypt to save them from Assyria ([Isaiah 30:2](https://ref.ly/Isa30:2)). 

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 28:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.8`); Work (ID: `keyterm:Work`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Mighty (ID: `keyterm:Mighty.2`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Word of the Lord (ID: `keyterm:WordOfTheLord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); Assyrians (ID: `group:Assyria`)

## Isaiah 28:2 (id: 16106)

* *the Lord has one who is*: The covenant God will act against his own people, whose behavior had effectively disqualified them as God’s people (see [Hosea 1:9](https://ref.ly/Hos1:9)).
* *one who is strong and mighty*: This refers to the Assyrians, who destroyed Samaria in 722 BC.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 28:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 28:5–6 (id: 16107)

In the future, after God’s judgment has purged his people, he will restore a righteous remnant, transforming the desires and character of his people. Samaria will no longer be the source of Israel’s pride. Instead, they will glory in the Lord, their true crown.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 28:5-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Crown (ID: `realia:Crown.2`); Crown (ID: `realia:Crown`)

## Isaiah 28:7 (id: 16109)

* The priests and prophets led the people astray.
* Intoxicating beverages such as wine and alcohol were prohibited for priests while fulfilling their duties ([Leviticus 10:9](https://ref.ly/Lev10:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 28:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`)

## Isaiah 28:7–13 (id: 16108)

The focus shifts back to the unfortunate current situation in Israel (and Judah). The leaders and people will be trapped by their own plans.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 28:7-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Word of the Lord (ID: `keyterm:WordOfTheLord`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Isaiah 28:9–10 (id: 16110)

The people had become resistant to God's message ([1 Corinthians 14:21](https://ref.ly/1Cor14:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 28:9-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Isaiah 28:11 (id: 16091)

* God will communicate through situations like hardship, exile, and death to reach his stubborn people.
* *foreign tongues*: In the New Testament, Paul used this text to describe the spiritual gift of tongues as a sign of judgment for unbelievers (see [1 Corinthians 14:21–22](https://ref.ly/1Cor14:21-1Cor14:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 28:11


## Isaiah 28:12 (id: 16092)

If his people had listened, God's revelation would have led them to rest (see [Isaiah 48:18–19](https://ref.ly/Isa48:18-Isa48:19)). However, they did not listen because of their stubborn hearts ([Isaiah 65:12](https://ref.ly/Isa65:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 28:12


## Isaiah 28:13 (id: 16093)

*line on line, line on line; a little here, a little there*: The Hebrew text is hard to understand. The words might be intentional nonsense to illustrate the point that the people were so spiritually blind that God’s clearest revelation was nonsense to them (see [Isaiah 6:9–10](https://ref.ly/Isa6:9-Isa6:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 28:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Word of the Lord (ID: `keyterm:WordOfTheLord`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Isaiah 28:14–22 (id: 16094)

After giving judgment on Samaria ([Isaiah 28:1–13](https://ref.ly/Isa28:1-Isa28:13)), Isaiah now judges Jerusalem. He accuses Judah's leaders of being as blind as those in Israel's northern kingdom.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 28:14-22

## Isaiah 28:15 (id: 16095)

* *have made a covenant with death … fashioned an agreement with Sheol*: There are two possible interpretations:

    1. The people had pledged themselves to the Canaanite god of death, Mot, for his supposed protection.
        2. The prophet was sarcastic. The people believed their political and economic actions ensured their safety, but they had actually harmed themselves.
* *lies … and falsehood*: The leaders did not see themselves as deceptive, but they were.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 28:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Refuge (ID: `keyterm:Refuge`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Whip (ID: `realia:Whip`)

## Isaiah 28:16 (id: 16096)

The Lord is the dependable *cornerstone* in Jerusalem, a valuable foundation stone of God's true city. There would be real hope in Jerusalem if the leaders and people turned to Him. By trusting in God, they would be safe during the worst storm or earthquake (see [Isaiah 8:14](https://ref.ly/Isa8:14); [Psalm 118:22](https://ref.ly/Ps118:22); [Matthew 7:24–27](https://ref.ly/Matt7:24-Matt7:27); [21:42](https://ref.ly/Matt21:42); see the application to Jesus Christ, [Romans 9:33](https://ref.ly/Rom9:33); [10:11](https://ref.ly/Rom10:11); [1 Corinthians 3:11](https://ref.ly/1Cor3:11); [Ephesians 2:20](https://ref.ly/Eph2:20); [1 Peter 2:4–7](https://ref.ly/1Pet2:4-1Pet2:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 28:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Foundation (ID: `realia:Foundation`); Believe In (ID: `keyterm:BelieveIn`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Isaiah 28:17 (id: 16097)

This is the second of the six woes (*Woe …*).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:1-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`); Bring Honor and Glory on Oneself (ID: `keyterm:BringHonorAndGloryOnOneself`); Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); David (ID: `person:David`); Mighty (ID: `keyterm:Mighty.2`); Necromancer (ID: `keyterm:Spirit`); Siege Tower (ID: `realia:SiegeTower`); Siege-Works (ID: `realia:Siege-works`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Isaiah 29:3 (id: 16125)

The Lord himself would come against Jerusalem, surrounding and attacking it.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Siege Tower (ID: `realia:SiegeTower`); Siege-Works (ID: `realia:Siege-works`)

## Isaiah 29:4 (id: 16126)

God aimed to destroy the sinful pride of Jerusalem's people. Their voices would rise as if from the grave. Through the Assyrians, God would humble his people, but he would not abandon them.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Necromancer (ID: `keyterm:Spirit`)

## Isaiah 29:6 (id: 16127)

* *you will be visited*: God will rescue his people.
* *thunder and earthquake … tempest and consuming flame of fire*: These events describe a theophany (a visible appearance of God; see study note on Isaiah 5:25).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:6
* **Associated Articles:** Isaiah 5:25 (ID: `16462@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 29:7 (id: 16128)

The Assyrians suddenly ended the siege, as if in a dream or a vision in the night. God would save Judah and judge Assyria.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Isaiah 29:8 (id: 16130)

Like a hungry or thirsty person, the Assyrians could taste the victory over Jerusalem. Yet they never became victorious.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Isaiah 29:8–12 (id: 16129)

Jerusalem’s blindness kept its people from understanding God’s plan.

Here, *blindness* does not mean physical blindness. It means spiritual blindness. The people could see with their eyes, but they did not understand what God was doing.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:8-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Isaiah 29:10 (id: 16114)

* *a spirit of deep sleep*: God reinforced the people's foolishness as a judgment on them (compare [Romans 1:24–32](https://ref.ly/Rom1:24-Rom1:32); [11:8](https://ref.ly/Rom11:8)). They could not see reality.
* People sometimes called prophets visionaries. Neither the people nor their prophets understood what God was doing ([Isaiah 6:9–10](https://ref.ly/Isa6:9-Isa6:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 29:13 (id: 16115)

* These people used religious language in their prayers and conversations (see [Matthew 15:8](https://ref.ly/Matt15:8); [Mark 7:6–7](https://ref.ly/Mark7:6-Mark7:7)), but they did not truly honor God.
* In their hearts, they were not committed to the Lord.
* In their worship, they followed human rules and regulations instead of God's word.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bring Honor and Glory on Oneself (ID: `keyterm:BringHonorAndGloryOnOneself`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Isaiah 29:14 (id: 16116)

Human wisdom and intelligence will disappear (see [1 Corinthians 1:19](https://ref.ly/1Cor1:19)). The prophet had already spoken about the failure of Egypt’s wise men ([Isaiah 19:11–12](https://ref.ly/Isa19:11-Isa19:12)). Even Judah’s wise men would make mistakes.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`)

## Isaiah 29:15 (id: 16118)

* Their plans might refer to the advice that royal counselors were giving Hezekiah, who at first attempted to free himself from Assyria by making alliances with Egypt (in 715 or 701 BC).
* The people secretly planned evil deeds, but God saw everything.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`)

## Isaiah 29:15–24 (id: 16117)

This threat of coming judgment is the third of the six woes (see study note on Isaiah 28:1–33:24). It begins with judgment but moves to a vision of creation being renewed and of the wicked coming to an end.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:15-24
* **Associated Articles:** Isaiah 28:1-33.24 (ID: `16090@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); Poor (ID: `keyterm:Poor`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Mighty (ID: `keyterm:Mighty.2`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Isaiah 29:16 (id: 16119)

* *potter … clay*: This picture shows that the Lord has full authority over his creation. A potter has the right to shape clay as he chooses. In the same way, God has the right to guide and rule his people.

    Scripture does not forbid people from asking God honest and difficult questions. However, it does not allow people to resist or reject God’s will (see [Isaiah 10:15](https://ref.ly/Isa10:15); [45:9](https://ref.ly/Isa45:9); [64:8](https://ref.ly/Isa64:8); [Romans 9:20](https://ref.ly/Rom9:20)).

* *He did not make me?*: When people speak this way about God, they refuse to admit that God created them. They deny that God is closely involved in every part of their lives.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Isaiah 29:17 (id: 16120)

The forests of Lebanon usually picture rich and healthy growth (see [Isaiah 2:13](https://ref.ly/Isa2:13); [14:8](https://ref.ly/Isa14:8)). They are often a symbol of strength and beauty.

But here, the forests represent destruction and emptiness. Instead of life and growth, they show ruin and loss.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Isaiah 29:18 (id: 16121)

The people were deaf and blind in their hearts and spirits. This means they would not listen to God or understand his truth (see [Isaiah 6:10](https://ref.ly/Isa6:10); [42:18](https://ref.ly/Isa42:18); [43:8](https://ref.ly/Isa43:8)). 

Yet God promised that he would renew humanity and all creation. He would bring healing and new life (see [Isaiah 35:1–5](https://ref.ly/Isa35:1-Isa35:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Isaiah 29:21 (id: 16122)

The false testimony that led to the oppression of the poor through trickery in the courts of Isaiah’s era would end. Because of God’s work in their hearts, the people would turn from their sinful behavior of the past.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`)

## Isaiah 29:22 (id: 16124)

* Abraham was the father of all Israel (see [Genesis 12:1–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:1-Gen12:3); see also [Galatians 3:29](https://ref.ly/Gal3:29)).
* Saved from human mistreatment and God's judgment, the people would no longer feel ashamed. Their disgrace came from the failure of what they had trusted (see [Psalm 71:1](https://ref.ly/Ps71:1); [1 Peter 2:6](https://ref.ly/1Pet2:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`)

## Isaiah 29:22–24 (id: 16123)

The prophecy of woe, which began at [29:15](https://ref.ly/Isa29:15), now ends with a prophecy of salvation.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 29:22-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`)

## Isaiah 30:1 (id: 16158)

The people of Judah did not follow God's guidance (compare [Isaiah 11:2](https://ref.ly/Isa11:2)). They made alliances that God did not approve. The Assyrian attack on Jerusalem was overwhelming. By allying with Egypt, Judah's leaders showed they relied on human help instead of God. Fortunately, Hezekiah later changed his mind (see [Isaiah 36–37](https://ref.ly/Isa36:1-Isa37:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Isaiah 30:1–5 (id: 16148)

This prophecy warned Judah not to rely on Egypt. It is the fourth of six woes (see study note on Isaiah 28:1–33:24).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:1-5
* **Associated Articles:** Isaiah 28:1-33.24 (ID: `16090@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.8`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`); Hanes (ID: `place:Hanes`); Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Zoan (ID: `place:Zoan`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`)

## Isaiah 30:1–33 (id: 16147)

After warning Judah of judgment due to their stubbornness and reliance on Egypt ([Isaiah 30:1–17](https://ref.ly/Isa30:1-Isa30:17)), God promised to show compassion to his people and defeat their enemies.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:1-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.8`); Hanes (ID: `place:Hanes`); Fortress (ID: `realia:Fortress`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Topheth (ID: `place:Topheth`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Zoan (ID: `place:Zoan`); Assyrians (ID: `group:Assyria`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Seek Refugue (ID: `keyterm:SeekfindRefuge`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); Have a Vision (ID: `keyterm:HaveAVision`); Utterance (ID: `keyterm:Utterance`); Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Bit and Bridle (ID: `realia:BitAndBridle`); Chickpea (ID: `flora:Chickpea`); Dragon (ID: `fauna:Dragon`); Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Viper (ID: `fauna:Viper`); Drum (ID: `realia:Drum`); Tablet (ID: `realia:Tablet`); Flute (ID: `realia:Flute`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`); Standard (ID: `realia:Standard`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Winnowing Fork (ID: `realia:WinnowingFork`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`); Sieve (ID: `realia:Sieve`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Clay Jar (ID: `realia:ClayJar`)

## Isaiah 30:4 (id: 16170)

*Zoan*: See [Isaiah 19:11](https://ref.ly/Isa19:11). The location of Hanes is uncertain.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hanes (ID: `place:Hanes`); Zoan (ID: `place:Zoan`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Isaiah 30:6 (id: 16172)

* Caravans traveled from Judah through the Negev and the Sinai Peninsula to Egypt. They avoided the main road along the coast because Assyria controlled it.

    This desert region was dangerous. Lions and poisonous snakes lived there. The journey put people’s lives at risk. Yet they were willing to face this danger to form an alliance that would not help them.

* *they carry their wealth … and their treasures*: This may refer to money and valuable goods that Judah sent to Egypt. They likely sent these riches to pay for Egypt’s protection and support.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Utterance (ID: `keyterm:Utterance`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); Dragon (ID: `fauna:Dragon`); Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`); Viper (ID: `fauna:Viper`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`)

## Isaiah 30:6–7 (id: 16171)

This taunt was against those who were busy trying to please the Egyptians in order to get Egypt’s military support.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Utterance (ID: `keyterm:Utterance`); Negev (ID: `place:Negeb`); Dragon (ID: `fauna:Dragon`); Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`); Viper (ID: `fauna:Viper`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`)

## Isaiah 30:7 (id: 16173)

*Rahab Who Sits Still*: In ancient mythology, Rahab was an enemy of the good gods and appeared as a fearsome creature, similar to a giant crocodile (see [Isaiah 27:1](https://ref.ly/Isa27:1); [51:9](https://ref.ly/Isa51:9); [Job 41:1–34](https://ref.ly/Job41:1-Job41:34)). By calling Egypt *futile,* the prophet meant it would not help Judah.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Isaiah 30:8–11 (id: 16174)

God instructed the prophet to write down the vision for future generations. The present generation was rebellious. They chose false prophets' fantasies over God's word (see[Isaiah 8:16–17](https://ref.ly/Isa8:16-Isa8:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:8-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Have a Vision (ID: `keyterm:HaveAVision`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Tablet (ID: `realia:Tablet`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Isaiah 30:11 (id: 16149)

*Rid us*: The people of Judah did not want to be confronted about sin or judgment.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 30:12 (id: 16151)

The whole society accepted injustice and lies. People encouraged one another to believe things that were not true.

They believed that Egypt would protect them from Assyria, even though that was false. Because many people accepted this idea, it seemed normal and wise to trust Egypt.

It also became accepted to take land from the poor. Leaders treated this oppression as if it were legal and right.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 30:12–17 (id: 16150)

Judgment would suddenly overtake the people of Judah because they were content with their fantasy world, and they refused God’s path to rest and quietness.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:12-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`); Standard (ID: `realia:Standard`); Clay Jar (ID: `realia:ClayJar`)

## Isaiah 30:15 (id: 16152)

* The people of Judah needed to turn away from their sinful ways ([Isaiah 30:12–14](https://ref.ly/Isa30:12-Isa30:14)) and return to the Lord to be saved.
* Trusting in the Lord would bring peace and confidence, unlike their desperate talks with Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 30:16 (id: 16153)

Judah relied on Egypt for fast horses (see [Psalm 33:17](https://ref.ly/Ps33:17)), rejecting God's help and risking its downfall.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`)

## Isaiah 30:17 (id: 16154)

* *one … five*: This curse is the opposite of God's blessing for obedience ([Leviticus 26:8](https://ref.ly/Lev26:8); [Deuteronomy 32:30](https://ref.ly/Deut32:30)).
* The Assyrians had taken control of most of the land of Judah. Only Jerusalem remained.

    The city stood alone, like a single pole on top of a hill. It was isolated and surrounded by enemy territory.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Standard (ID: `realia:Standard`)

## Isaiah 30:18 (id: 16156)

* A faithful God will restore order to the world by punishing the wicked and saving his people from them (see [Isaiah 1:27](https://ref.ly/Isa1:27); [Psalm 96:10–12](https://ref.ly/Ps96:10-Ps96:12)).
* God blesses those who wait. Faithful people do not rush to solve their own problems. Instead, they rely on his power and goodness.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 30:18–33 (id: 16155)

This prophecy of salvation gave promises of hope. It helped Israel look away from their present suffering.

Instead, it pointed them toward the glorious future that God had prepared for his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:18-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Topheth (ID: `place:Topheth`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Assyrians (ID: `group:Assyria`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Bit and Bridle (ID: `realia:BitAndBridle`); Chickpea (ID: `flora:Chickpea`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Drum (ID: `realia:Drum`); Flute (ID: `realia:Flute`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Winnowing Fork (ID: `realia:WinnowingFork`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`); Sieve (ID: `realia:Sieve`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`)

## Isaiah 30:19 (id: 16157)

* This was likely a challenge and promise to Hezekiah during the Assyrian siege of Jerusalem (see [Isaiah 36–37](https://ref.ly/Isa36:1-Isa37:38)). If he trusted in God, his people would not weep anymore.
* The Lord cares for his people's needs. When they ask for something, he will surely respond.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Isaiah 30:20 (id: 16159)

*adversity and the water of affliction*: A city under enemy siege for a long time would eventually face starvation and disease. God did not intend for this disaster to destroy the people of Jerusalem. Instead, it was meant to teach them.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 30:21 (id: 16160)

The people’s own ears will hear and follow God’s instructions (see [Isaiah 6:9–10](https://ref.ly/Isa6:9-Isa6:10); [29:18](https://ref.ly/Isa29:18); [35:5](https://ref.ly/Isa35:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:21


## Isaiah 30:22 (id: 16161)

One aspect of Hezekiah’s reforms was to remove silver idols and other idolatry of Ahaz’s era and to call the people back to the worship of the God of Israel ([2 Kings 18:4–5](https://ref.ly/2Kgs18:4-2Kgs18:5); [2 Chronicles 29](https://ref.ly/2Chr29:1-2Chr29:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`)

## Isaiah 30:23–24 (id: 16162)

The people of Judah would again enjoy covenant blessings rather than curses (see [Leviticus 26:4](https://ref.ly/Lev26:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:23-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Chickpea (ID: `flora:Chickpea`); Force to Serve (ID: `keyterm:EnticedToServe`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`); Winnowing Fork (ID: `realia:WinnowingFork`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`)

## Isaiah 30:26 (id: 16163)

The Lord who struck his people would also heal them and cure the wounds he gave them.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 30:27–33 (id: 16164)

In this prophecy of judgment against the nations, Isaiah foretold that Assyria’s power would be broken by God’s strong arm, not by the Egyptians.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:27-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Assyrians (ID: `group:Assyria`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Topheth (ID: `place:Topheth`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Bit and Bridle (ID: `realia:BitAndBridle`); Drum (ID: `realia:Drum`); Flute (ID: `realia:Flute`); Sieve (ID: `realia:Sieve`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`)

## Isaiah 30:28 (id: 16165)

*like a rushing torrent that rises to the neck*: Assyria’s destruction would be as devastating as their invasion of Judah (see [Isaiah 8:8](https://ref.ly/Isa8:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bit and Bridle (ID: `realia:BitAndBridle`); Sieve (ID: `realia:Sieve`)

## Isaiah 30:30 (id: 16166)

* *His mighty arm*: This phrase is a picture of great strength. It reminds readers of how God defeated the Egyptians during the exodus from Egypt ([Isaiah 63:12](https://ref.ly/Isa63:12); see [Exodus 15:6](https://ref.ly/Exod15:6)).
* *consuming fire … hailstones*: Fire and hail often appear when God shows himself in power. These signs are connected with a theophany (a visible appearance of God). They show that God is acting in judgment and authority (see study note on Isaiah 5:25).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:30
* **Associated Articles:** Isaiah 5:25 (ID: `16462@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Majesty (ID: `keyterm:Majesty`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 30:31 (id: 16167)

*scepter*: The Lord, the true King, would be victorious over the threatening Assyrian king.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Assyrians (ID: `group:Assyria`)

## Isaiah 30:32 (id: 16168)

*tambourines and lyres*: Instruments once silenced in judgment ([Isaiah 24:8](https://ref.ly/Isa24:8)) would be used again by God's people. This time, they would celebrate the Lord's goodness instead of becoming spiritually complacent (see [Isaiah 5:12](https://ref.ly/Isa5:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Drum (ID: `realia:Drum`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`)

## Isaiah 30:33 (id: 16169)

Topheth was a pagan altar in the Valley of Ben\-hinnom, outside Jerusalem. At this place, some people burned their children as sacrifices to the Ammonite god Molech. This was part of false worship ([2 Kings 23:9–10](https://ref.ly/2Kgs23:9-2Kgs23:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 30:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Topheth (ID: `place:Topheth`)

## Isaiah 31:1 (id: 16176)

* *Woe*: This is the fifth of the six woes (see study note on Isaiah 28:1–33:24).
* *seek the LORD*: This suggests seeking God's guidance with a worshipful attitude (see [Deuteronomy 4:29](https://ref.ly/Deut4:29); [Psalm 105:3–4](https://ref.ly/Ps105:3-Ps105:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 31:1
* **Associated Articles:** Isaiah 28:1-33.24 (ID: `16090@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`)

## Isaiah 31:1–3 (id: 16175)

Isaiah delivered another vision regarding Judah’s negotiations with Egypt, which probably took place in 705–701 BC. During that time, the conquering Assyrians were on the move, threatening Judah. At first, Hezekiah tried political and military means to avert being totally conquered (see [2 Kings 18:13–16](https://ref.ly/2Kgs18:13-2Kgs18:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 31:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`)

## Isaiah 31:4–9 (id: 16177)

This prophecy concerned God’s protection of Jerusalem and its rescue from the Assyrians.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 31:4-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Assyrians (ID: `group:Assyria`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Idol (ID: `keyterm:Idol`); Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Standard (ID: `realia:Standard`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`)

## Isaiah 31:9 (id: 16178)

* *princes will panic*: This happened when Sennacherib ran to Nineveh after God destroyed 185,000 troops (see[Isaiah 37:36–37](https://ref.ly/Isa37:36-Isa37:37)).
* God was the fire or flame that would consume Assyria.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 31:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Standard (ID: `realia:Standard`)

## Isaiah 32:1 (id: 16186)

* *a king will reign in righteousness*: This means the Messiah, God's chosen one ([Isaiah 11:1](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1)). The focus is now on righteous and wise leadership ([Proverbs 8:20](https://ref.ly/Prov8:20)).
* In Isaiah’s time, honest leaders were rare (see [Isaiah 3:1–6](https://ref.ly/Isa3:1-Isa3:6); [28:7–19](https://ref.ly/Isa28:7-Isa28:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:1


## Isaiah 32:2 (id: 16187)

*like a shelter … a refuge*: These words also describe God's protection ([Isaiah 25:4](https://ref.ly/Isa25:4)) and his gift of life\-giving water ([41:18](https://ref.ly/Isa41:18); [43:19–20](https://ref.ly/Isa43:19-Isa43:20)). The leaders would act as God's agents to bless his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:2


## Isaiah 32:5 (id: 16188)

Only foolish people value fools and scoundrels as heroes (see also [Isaiah 19:11](https://ref.ly/Isa19:11), [13](https://ref.ly/Isa19:13); [32:6–8](https://ref.ly/Isa32:6-Isa32:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:5


## Isaiah 32:6 (id: 16189)

Fools ignore God's standards of justice and righteousness. They do not care about people who are hungry or thirsty (see [Isaiah 1:17](https://ref.ly/Isa1:17); [58:7](https://ref.ly/Isa58:7), [10](https://ref.ly/Isa58:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Foolish (ID: `keyterm:Foolish`)

## Isaiah 32:7 (id: 16190)

*weapons … plots*: See [Proverbs 6:12](https://ref.ly/Prov6:12), [18](https://ref.ly/Prov6:18); [16:27](https://ref.ly/Prov16:27); [24:8–9](https://ref.ly/Prov24:8-Prov24:9).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:7


## Isaiah 32:9 (id: 16192)

The women of Jerusalem live comfortably. They rely on wealth and status to keep their lifestyle, despite having low morals.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`)

## Isaiah 32:9–15 (id: 16191)

This prophecy warned the complacent women of Jerusalem. God's promises for the future did not excuse ongoing sin.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:9-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Watch-Tower (ID: `realia:Watch-tower`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Wild Donkey (ID: `fauna:WildDonkey`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`)

## Isaiah 32:10 (id: 16179)

*In a little more than a year*: This refers to events that were about to happen soon.

At that time, the Assyrian Empire was preparing to attack Jerusalem. The siege of Jerusalem in 701 BC was approaching.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Isaiah 32:11 (id: 16180)

People wore sackcloth to show grief, especially during mourning (see [Psalm 30:11](https://ref.ly/Ps30:11)). The Assyrian conquest of Judah's farmlands and most cities, except Jerusalem, would cause wealthy city dwellers to mourn.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`)

## Isaiah 32:14 (id: 16181)

The Babylonians, not the Assyrians, eventually destroyed Jerusalem (see [Isaiah 39:1–8](https://ref.ly/Isa39:1-Isa39:8) for a prediction of this).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Watch-Tower (ID: `realia:Watch-tower`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Wild Donkey (ID: `fauna:WildDonkey`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Isaiah 32:15 (id: 16182)

* The Spirit will change the nation into a godly community, starting a time of justice and righteousness (see also [Joel 2:28–32](https://ref.ly/Joel2:28-Joel2:32)). The Spirit is connected to the Messiah, God's chosen one ([Isaiah 11:1](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1)) and the servant ([42:1](https://ref.ly/Isa42:1); [61:1](https://ref.ly/Isa61:1)).
* *desert … orchard*: When the people are transformed, nature will be too.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Isaiah 32:17 (id: 16183)

Being right with God and people will bring peace (Hebrew *shalom*; see [Isaiah 26:3](https://ref.ly/Isa26:3); [48:18](https://ref.ly/Isa48:18); [52:7](https://ref.ly/Isa52:7); compare [James 3:18](https://ref.ly/Jas3:18)). Peace is more than just the absence of conflict. It includes personal wholeness and does not rely on outside circumstances (see also [Romans 5:1](https://ref.ly/Rom5:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Work (ID: `keyterm:Work`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`)

## Isaiah 32:18 (id: 16184)

Living safely and feeling at peace were blessings of Israel's agreement with the Lord (see [Leviticus 26:5–6](https://ref.ly/Lev26:5-Lev26:6)). However, the people's sins had brought curses instead of blessings (compare [Isaiah 32:9–11](https://ref.ly/Isa32:9-Isa32:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`)

## Isaiah 32:19 (id: 16185)

*the forest, and the city*: The faithful will stay safe, even when signs of God's judgment are everywhere.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 32:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Isaiah 33:1 (id: 16200)

* This was the sixth warning of woe (see study note on Isaiah 28:1–33:24).
* Although the Hebrew text does not specifically name Assyria as the object of the prophecy in this chapter, Isaiah likely referred to Assyria. They were the *destroyer* closest at the time the prophecy was written. The prophecy applies to anyone who tries to destroy God's people, including the Babylonians (see [Isaiah 13–14](https://ref.ly/Isa13:1-Isa14:32)).
* *When you have finished betraying*: Wicked nations often break political agreements when these deals no longer benefit them.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 33:1
* **Associated Articles:** Isaiah 28:1-33.24 (ID: `16090@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`)

## Isaiah 33:2 (id: 16202)

The faithful community prayed for God to be merciful in response to the promise of [Isaiah 30:18](https://ref.ly/Isa30:18).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 33:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Isaiah 33:9 (id: 16203)

Sharon, Bashan, and Carmel were all fertile areas. Their desolation represents the destruction Assyria had caused in Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 33:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sharon Plain (ID: `place:Sharon.2`); Carmel (ID: `place:Carmel`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); Bashan (ID: `place:Bashan`); Arabah (ID: `place:Arabah`)

## Isaiah 33:11 (id: 16193)

In most passages, the Assyrians reduced other nations to worthless dry grass and stubble (see [Isaiah 17:13](https://ref.ly/Isa17:13); [29:5](https://ref.ly/Isa29:5); [40:24](https://ref.ly/Isa40:24); [41:2](https://ref.ly/Isa41:2)). Here, ironically, they experience the same fate.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 33:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Isaiah 33:14 (id: 16194)

* The godless were people who lived without considering God and his laws.
* The image of a consuming fire comes from God’s judgment of wrongdoers during the wilderness journey ([Exodus 24:17](https://ref.ly/Exod24:17); [Deuteronomy 4:24](https://ref.ly/Deut4:24)). God still expects people to worship him with respect and awe ([Hebrews 12:29](https://ref.ly/Heb12:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 33:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sinners (ID: `group:Sinners`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Isaiah 33:15 (id: 16195)

Living a righteous life shows that a person truly knows God (see [Colossians 3:5–17](https://ref.ly/Col3:5-Col3:17); [James 2:14–18](https://ref.ly/Jas2:14-Jas2:18); [1 Peter 1:14–16](https://ref.ly/1Pet1:14-1Pet1:16); [1 John 1:5–6](https://ref.ly/1John1:5-1John1:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 33:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`)

## Isaiah 33:17 (id: 16197)

* The king is the human representative ([32:1](https://ref.ly/Isa32:1)) of the Great King, God himself ([33:22](https://ref.ly/Isa33:22)).
* The land belonging to God’s people would appear to be without borders because the foreign enemies had been judged ([54:3](https://ref.ly/Isa54:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 33:17


## Isaiah 33:17–24 (id: 16196)

This vision is about God’s reign in Zion.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 33:17-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Rope (ID: `realia:Rope`); Terror (ID: `keyterm:Terror`); Mighty (ID: `keyterm:Mighty`); Appointed Feast (ID: `keyterm:AppointedFeast`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); A Person (ID: `person:GenericPerson`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Mast (ID: `realia:Mast`); Sail (ID: `realia:Sail`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`); Peg (ID: `realia:Peg`); Peg (ID: `realia:Peg.2`)

## Isaiah 33:18 (id: 16198)

Assyrian officers brought great terror to Judah when the Assyrians defeated various cities around Jerusalem ([2 Kings 18:13](https://ref.ly/2Kgs18:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 33:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Terror (ID: `keyterm:Terror`); A Person (ID: `person:GenericPerson`); Tower (ID: `realia:Tower`)

## Isaiah 33:19 (id: 16199)

The strange, unknown language was the language spoken in Assyria and later in Babylon (see [Isaiah 28:11](https://ref.ly/Isa28:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 33:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`)

## Isaiah 33:24 (id: 16201)

* The people of Israel were the people of Zion (compare [Isaiah 33:20](https://ref.ly/Isa33:20)).
* *sick …* *will be forgiven of iniquity*: See [Isaiah 43:25](https://ref.ly/Isa43:25).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 33:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`)

This prophecy of judgment was against the nations at the terrifying day of the Lord (see study note on Isaiah 2:5–22; see also[Isaiah 63:1–6](https://ref.ly/Isa63:1-Isa63:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 34:1-17
* **Associated Articles:** Isaiah 2:5-22 (ID: `15969@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Isaiah 34:2 (id: 16209)

*devote them to destruction*: See [Leviticus 27:28–29](https://ref.ly/Lev27:28-Lev27:29).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 34:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Set Apart for God (ID: `keyterm:Proscribe`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 34:3 (id: 16210)

Leaving a dead body unburied was shameful.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 34:3


## Isaiah 34:4 (id: 16211)

*The skies … stars*: See also [Isaiah 13:10](https://ref.ly/Isa13:10); [Joel 2:30–31](https://ref.ly/Joel2:30-Joel2:31); [Matthew 24:29](https://ref.ly/Matt24:29); [Mark 13:24–25](https://ref.ly/Mark13:24-Mark13:25); [2 Peter 3:10–13](https://ref.ly/2Pet3:10-2Pet3:13); [Revelation 6:13–14](https://ref.ly/Rev6:13-Rev6:14).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 34:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`)

## Isaiah 34:5 (id: 16212)

Edom represents what all nations will experience, similar to how Moab was described in [Isaiah 25](https://ref.ly/Isa25:1-Isa25:12).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 34:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Edom (ID: `place:Edom`); Something Devoted to God (ID: `keyterm:SomethingDevotedToGod`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`)

## Isaiah 34:6 (id: 16213)

Bozrah was a city in Edom, southeast of the Dead Sea.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 34:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bozrah (ID: `place:Bozrah.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Edom (ID: `place:Edom`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`)

## Isaiah 34:8 (id: 16214)

* The Lord’s revenge means God’s just anger against his enemies and those who oppress his people on the day of the Lord.
* *a year of recompense*: There will be payback for Edom’s wrongs against Judah (see also [Psalm 137:7](https://ref.ly/Ps137:7); [Lamentations 4:21](https://ref.ly/Lam4:21); Obadiah; [Malachi 1:3–5](https://ref.ly/Mal1:3-Mal1:5)). Edom, known for its betrayal, will meet its end.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 34:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Isaiah 34:9 (id: 16215)

Edom’s judgment was similar to the judgment of Sodom and Gomorrah ([Genesis 19:24](https://ref.ly/Gen19:24); see also [Jeremiah 49:17–18](https://ref.ly/Jer49:17-Jer49:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 34:9


## Isaiah 34:10 (id: 16205)

See also [Revelation 14:11](https://ref.ly/Rev14:11); [19:3](https://ref.ly/Rev19:3).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 34:10


## Isaiah 34:11 (id: 16207)

The identification of some of these birds is uncertain, but they inhabited wild and deserted places and many were considered unclean ([Deuteronomy 14:11–18](https://ref.ly/Deut14:11-Deut14:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 34:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jackdaw (ID: `fauna:Jackdaw`); Owl (ID: `fauna:Owl.3`); Tawny Owl (ID: `fauna:TawnyOwl`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Crow (ID: `fauna:Crow`); Measuring Reed (ID: `realia:MeasuringReed`)

## Isaiah 34:11–14 (id: 16206)

Wild animals, nocturnal birds, and thorny plants with thistles and briars filled the land. These things made the place feel empty and frightening.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 34:11-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Hyena (ID: `fauna:Hyena`); Jackdaw (ID: `fauna:Jackdaw`); Owl (ID: `fauna:Owl.3`); Tawny Owl (ID: `fauna:TawnyOwl`); Tawny Owl (ID: `fauna:TawnyOwl.2`); Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`); Nettle (ID: `flora:Nettle`); Crow (ID: `fauna:Crow`); Owl (ID: `fauna:Owl`); Ostrich (ID: `fauna:Ostrich`); Measuring Reed (ID: `realia:MeasuringReed`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`)

## Isaiah 34:16 (id: 16208)

* *Search and read the scroll of the LORD*: Isaiah's prophecy showed that God's word is true.
* The power of the Spirit makes the Lord's plans happen, ensuring all prophecy is fulfilled as God promised.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 34:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Isaiah 35:1 (id: 16218)

God's land would become a barren wasteland, both spiritually and physically. However, God would transform the desert into a garden (see also [Isaiah 32:15](https://ref.ly/Isa32:15)), bringing his people from exile to glory.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 35:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Narcissus (ID: `flora:Narcissus`)

## Isaiah 35:1–10 (id: 16216)

God will come to defend and change his people. They will no longer be deaf, blind, or lame (see [Isaiah 6:10](https://ref.ly/Isa6:10)). Instead, they will be pure, holy, and saved.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 35:1-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Way of Holiness (ID: `place:WayOfHoliness`); Sharon Plain (ID: `place:Sharon.2`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Carmel (ID: `place:Carmel`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); Fool (ID: `keyterm:Fool`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Narcissus (ID: `flora:Narcissus`); Reed (ID: `flora:Reed`); Lion (ID: `fauna:Lion`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Deer (ID: `fauna:Deer`); Papyrus (ID: `flora:Papyrus`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`)

## Isaiah 35:2 (id: 16219)

* *Lebanon … Carmel … Sharon*: There will be a reversal of earlier judgments (see [Isaiah 2:13](https://ref.ly/Isa2:13); [10:34](https://ref.ly/Isa10:34); [29:17](https://ref.ly/Isa29:17); [33:9](https://ref.ly/Isa33:9)).
* *glory … splendor of our God*: See also [Isaiah 2:3](https://ref.ly/Isa6:3); [40:5](https://ref.ly/Isa40:5).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 35:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sharon Plain (ID: `place:Sharon.2`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Carmel (ID: `place:Carmel`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`)

## Isaiah 35:3 (id: 16220)

*Limp hands* and *feeble knees* express discouragement and anxiety (see [Hebrews 12:12](https://ref.ly/Heb12:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 35:3


## Isaiah 35:4 (id: 16221)

* *do not fear!*: The Lord assures his people of his presence and purpose (see also [Isaiah 7:4](https://ref.ly/Isa7:4); [10:24](https://ref.ly/Isa10:24); [40:9](https://ref.ly/Isa40:9); [43:1](https://ref.ly/Isa43:1); [44:2](https://ref.ly/Isa44:2); [54:4](https://ref.ly/Isa54:4)).
* The main message of the good news is that God is coming to save his people (see also [Isaiah 40:9](https://ref.ly/Isa40:9); [52:7](https://ref.ly/Isa52:7); [62:11](https://ref.ly/Isa62:11)). God's arrival brings spiritual change and rescue from enemies.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 35:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Isaiah 35:5 (id: 16222)

*eyes … ears*: There will be a transformation from spiritual blindness and deafness to understanding and knowing God ([Isaiah 6:10](https://ref.ly/Isa6:10); [29:18](https://ref.ly/Isa29:18); [32:3](https://ref.ly/Isa32:3); [42:7](https://ref.ly/Isa42:7); see also [Luke 7:22](https://ref.ly/Luke7:22); [Acts 26:18](https://ref.ly/Acts26:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 35:5


## Isaiah 35:6 (id: 16223)

*the lame … and the mute*: This promise shows that God will remove all disabilities. He will bring healing and wholeness (see [Matthew 11:5](https://ref.ly/Matt11:5); [12:22](https://ref.ly/Matt12:22); [Mark 7:37](https://ref.ly/Mark7:37); [Acts 3:7–8](https://ref.ly/Acts3:7-Acts3:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 35:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Deer (ID: `fauna:Deer`)

## Isaiah 35:8 (id: 16224)

* The image of the highway recalls Israel’s crossing through the Red Sea ([Isaiah 51:10](https://ref.ly/Isa51:10)).
* *only those who walk*: This means that only those who follow God will walk on his holy path. God's goal in salvation is for humans to share his character and act like him (see [Ephesians 5:1–2](https://ref.ly/Eph5:1-Eph5:2); [1 Peter 2:21](https://ref.ly/1Pet2:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 35:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Way of Holiness (ID: `place:WayOfHoliness`); Fool (ID: `keyterm:Fool`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`)

## Isaiah 35:10 (id: 16217)

*Zion*: The prophecy goes beyond Israel's return from exile to the final establishment of God's kingdom on earth. This kingdom will be full of joy and happiness, with no sorrow or mourning (see also [Isaiah 25:7](https://ref.ly/Isa25:7); [51:11](https://ref.ly/Isa51:11); [Revelation 21:4](https://ref.ly/Rev21:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 35:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Isaiah 36:1 (id: 16233)

King Sennacherib of Assyria ruled from 705 to 681 BC. Hezekiah, like many other kings under Assyrian control, declared his independence when Sennacherib became king during a difficult time. By 701 BC, Sennacherib was ready to punish Hezekiah. Sennacherib claimed he conquered 46 fortified cities and many villages, taking 200,146 captives. Hezekiah tried to appease Sennacherib (see [2 Kings 18:14–16](https://ref.ly/2Kgs18:14-2Kgs18:16)), but it was too late.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 36:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hezekiah (ID: `person:Hezekiah`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Sennacherib (ID: `person:Sennacherib`)

## Isaiah 36:1–39.8 (id: 16225)

Jerusalem’s rescue from King Sennacherib of Assyria is a main event in [Isaiah 1–39](https://ref.ly/Isa1:1-Isa39:8). The Lord promised to save his people. He would remain present with Jerusalem (see [7:14](https://ref.ly/Isa7:14)). He would also keep a remnant, a small group who would survive (see study note on Isaiah 10:20). 

Much of the land became empty and the population greatly reduced. This happened because Judah made a foolish alliance with Assyria (chapters [7–8](https://ref.ly/Isa7:1-Isa8:22)). Even so, the Lord did not allow total defeat. He preserved his people through Hezekiah, a faithful king.

Hezekiah faced the same kind of test that King Ahaz had faced. It was a test of trust in the Lord. In chapter [36](https://ref.ly/Isa36:1-Isa36:22), the Assyrian officer repeats the word *trust* as he challenges the people. But Hezekiah had learned from the earlier warnings in chapters [13–35](https://ref.ly/Isa13:1-Isa35:10). At least at first, he trusted God and passed the test.

This story also appears in [2 Kings 18:13–20:19](https://ref.ly/2Kgs18:13-2Kgs20:19).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 36:1-37:38
* **Associated Articles:** Isaiah 10:20 (ID: `15824@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Hezekiah (ID: `person:Hezekiah`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`); Rabshakeh (ID: `person:Rabshakeh`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Shebna (ID: `person:Shebna`); Sennacherib (ID: `person:Sennacherib`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Eliakim (ID: `person:Eliakim.3`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Joah (ID: `person:Joah`); Amoz (ID: `person:Amoz`); Word of the Lord (ID: `keyterm:WordOfTheLord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Asaph (ID: `person:Asaph`); Sepharvaim (ID: `place:Sepharvaim`); Arpad (ID: `place:Arpad`); Hilkiah (ID: `person:Hilkiah`); David (ID: `person:David`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Lachish (ID: `place:Lachish`); Hamath (ID: `place:Hamath`)

## Isaiah 36:2 (id: 16234)

* Lachish was a city overlooking the hills west of Jerusalem. It needed to be captured before attacking Jerusalem.
* The aqueduct that supplies water to the upper pool was on the north side of Jerusalem, not the Gihon Spring in the Kidron Valley.
* Isaiah met Ahaz on this road leading to the field where cloth is washed about 33 years earlier (see [Isaiah 7:3](https://ref.ly/Isa7:3)). At that time, he urged Ahaz to trust God. However, Ahaz trusted Assyria instead, leading to this desperate situation. Now, the challenge to trust God came from mocking foreign voices (see [Isaiah 28:11–13](https://ref.ly/Isa28:11-Isa28:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 36:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Launderer’s Field (ID: `place:WashersField`); Lachish (ID: `place:Lachish`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Hezekiah (ID: `person:Hezekiah`); Rabshakeh (ID: `person:Rabshakeh`)

## Isaiah 36:4–22 (id: 16236)

The Assyrian chief of staff tried to use intimidation to negotiate a peaceful settlement. In his first speech ([Isaiah 36:4–10](https://ref.ly/Isa36:4-Isa36:10)), he correctly argued that Egypt could not rescue them but incorrectly accused Hezekiah of misplaced trust in the Lord. 

Interestingly, the Assyrian did not view the contest as between the gods of Assyria and the Lord, but rather between Sennacherib (the great king) and the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 36:4-22
