## Isaiah 36:6 (id: 16239)

* *Such is Pharaoh king of Egypt to all who trust in him*: Isaiah argued the same case, pointing to the Lord as the only reliable source of help ([Isaiah 31:1–3](https://ref.ly/Isa31:1-Isa31:3), [7](https://ref.ly/Isa31:7); [39:7](https://ref.ly/Isa39:7)).
* A kind of reed that breaks easily grows near the Nile (see [Ezekiel 29:6–7](https://ref.ly/Ezek29:6-Ezek29:7)).

## Isaiah 36:7 (id: 16240)

* *We trust in the LORD our God*: Ironically, the Assyrian chief of staff knew about Isaiah's message and used it to his advantage ([Isaiah 26:4](https://ref.ly/Isa26:4); [36:15](https://ref.ly/Isa36:15); [37:10](https://ref.ly/Isa37:10)).
* Hezekiah showed his dedication to the Lord by tearing down the pagan shrines and altars ([2 Kings 18:4](https://ref.ly/2Kgs18:4)). However, the Assyrians mistakenly thought Hezekiah had angered God by doing this.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 36:7

At this time, cavalry mounted on horses was the newest military technology. The Assyrian chief of staff was mocking the Judeans because he knew that Judah had no trained men who knew how to ride.

Hezekiah showed strong faith in the Lord, unlike Ahaz ([Isaiah 7](https://ref.ly/Isa7:1-Isa7:25)). Hezekiah’s faith led to rescue, while Ahaz’s lack of faith led to trouble (see [Isaiah 7:9](https://ref.ly/Isa7:9)).

## Isaiah 36:16–17 (id: 16231)

The Assyrian tried to lure the people of Jerusalem with supplies that the Lord promised to give to his obedient followers (see [Isaiah 1:19](https://ref.ly/Isa1:19); [37:30–35](https://ref.ly/Isa37:30-Isa37:35); [Deuteronomy 8:7–9](https://ref.ly/Deut8:7-Deut8:9)). It was a tempting offer, but it was fake. It hid the truth of the exile that would have come after ([Isaiah 36:17](https://ref.ly/Isa36:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 36:16-17

## Isaiah 36:19 (id: 16232)

The people of Hamath and Arpad were moved to Samaria, where they worshipped the gods of Sepharvaim ([2 Kings 17:24](https://ref.ly/2Kgs17:24)). This argument made sense to an Assyrian. If the Assyrian gods defeated the gods of other nations and the God of the Israelites in Samaria, would they not also defeat the God of Judah?

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 36:19

When Hezekiah received Sennacherib’s boastful threat, he went back to the temple and prayed for help. His prayer was different from Ahaz’s reaction to danger decades earlier (see [Isaiah 7](https://ref.ly/Isa7:1-Isa7:25)).

## Isaiah 37:16 (id: 16244)

* *God of Israel*: Hezekiah did not believe Sennacherib's false claims that the Lord was powerless. In his prayer, Hezekiah recognized that the Lord, as the creator, is the God of all the earth's kingdoms. The Lord rules over all kingdoms.
* The mighty cherubim were in the temple on top of the ark of the covenant, which was God's throne in his temple (see study note on Leviticus 16:2; see also [Psalms 80:1](https://ref.ly/Ps80:1); [99:1](https://ref.ly/Ps99:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 37:16

## Isaiah 37:20 (id: 16246)

*You alone, O LORD, are God*: Hezekiah cared about the Lord's reputation, which Sennacherib had insulted. Hezekiah understood that if the Lord defeated the Assyrians and their gods, His holy name would be honored worldwide (see [Exodus 9:16](https://ref.ly/Exod9:16); [14:4](https://ref.ly/Exod14:4), [17–18](https://ref.ly/Exod14:17-Exod14:18); [Joshua 2:9–11](https://ref.ly/Josh2:9-Josh2:11); [1 Samuel 4:7–8](https://ref.ly/1Sam4:7-1Sam4:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 37:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

The Lord answered him with a message of hope.

Isaiah seems to be familiar with the typical royal Assyrian boasts, including that they possessed the highest mountains. These words sound very much like those found in the annals of the Assyrian kings inscribed on the walls of their temples.

*your raging against Me*: Assyria was completely hostile to the Lord, which led to even greater condemnation for them.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 37:28


## Isaiah 37:29 (id: 16254)

*My hook … My bit*: The Assyrians would be taken away like animals, just as they had done to many of their captives.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 37:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Harpoon (ID: `realia:Harpoon`); Bit and Bridle (ID: `realia:BitAndBridle`)

## Isaiah 37:30 (id: 16256)

*This year … the second year … the third year*: Due to the Assyrian siege and its impact on farming, the people of Judah could not plant and harvest as usual. The promise that life would return to normal after the siege reassured them that God was with them and would provide for their daily needs. They needed to build their trust in God over three years, believing that God's word would come true (see [Isaiah 30:15](https://ref.ly/Isa30:15), [18](https://ref.ly/Isa30:18)). 

The idea was that after escaping the Assyrians, it would be too late to plant in the current year. They would have to wait until the fall of the second year to plant again and would harvest their first crop in the spring and summer of the third year.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 37:30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`)

## Isaiah 37:30–35 (id: 16255)

In this prophecy of salvation, Isaiah assured Hezekiah that Jerusalem would be spared and that the remnant was under God’s protection. 

The names of Isaiah and his sons anticipated God's rescue (see [Isaiah 7:1–11:16](https://ref.ly/Isa7:1-Isa11:16)). The book’s record of God’s presence and rescue provided assurance that the Lord would always have a remnant that he will protect and rescue.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 37:30-35

## Isaiah 37:35 (id: 16259)

*for the sake of My servant David*: God had made a promise to David. He said that David’s royal family would continue forever (see [Isaiah 9:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa9:6-Isa9:7); [2 Samuel 7:8–17](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:8-2Sam7:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 37:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`)

## Isaiah 37:36 (id: 16260)

* The angel of the Lord was a special heavenly messenger through whom God acted on earth. Often, he delivered important messages (see [Genesis 16:7–14](https://ref.ly/Gen16:7-Gen16:14)), but sometimes he brought judgment (see [2 Samuel 24:16](https://ref.ly/2Sam24:16)).
* *struck down 185,000 men*: The Lord began to fulfill his repeated promises: rescue of Jerusalem and judgment of Assyria (see [Isaiah 10:16](https://ref.ly/Isa10:16), [33–34](https://ref.ly/Isa10:33-Isa10:34); [30:31](https://ref.ly/Isa30:31); [31:8](https://ref.ly/Isa31:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 37:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Assyrians (ID: `group:Assyria`)

## Isaiah 37:38 (id: 16261)

* *One day*: Sennacherib was likely killed in 681 BC, about twenty years after he left Jerusalem.
* Esarhaddon ruled Assyria from 680 to 669 BC. It is ironic that Sennacherib, who mocked the Lord, was killed by his sons in the temple of his own god.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 37:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Adrammelech (ID: `person:Adrammelech.2`); Esarhaddon (ID: `person:Esarhaddon`); Sharezer (ID: `person:Sharezer`); Nisroch (ID: `deity:Nisroch`); Ararat (ID: `place:Ararat`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Isaiah 38:1 (id: 16272)

I*n those days*: This is a very general time statement. Hezekiah’s illness and healing likely happened before Jerusalem’s rescue ([Isaiah 36–37](https://ref.ly/Isa36:1-Isa37:38)), even though Isaiah mentions it afterward. Merodach\-baladan’s rule in Babylon ([Isaiah 39:1](https://ref.ly/Isa39:1)) ended before Sennacherib’s invasion in 701 BC. The text order suggests a link between these events (see [Isaiah 38:6](https://ref.ly/Isa38:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 38:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hezekiah (ID: `person:Hezekiah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Amoz (ID: `person:Amoz`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`)

## Isaiah 38:1–9 (id: 16266)

The story of Hezekiah’s healing connects two events. It links the extension of Hezekiah’s life with Jerusalem’s rescue from the Assyrians.

These events happened before Jerusalem was saved. However, the book places them in this order because of their theme, not their time sequence. One main theme is the power of prayer (see [Isaiah 37:14–20](https://ref.ly/Isa37:14-Isa37:20); [38:2–3](https://ref.ly/Isa38:2-Isa38:3)). The Lord truly listens.

Hezekiah learned to trust God. His father, Ahaz, had refused to trust the Lord. Because Hezekiah trusted God, Judah and Jerusalem were spared complete destruction by the Assyrians.

Still, Hezekiah was not the promised Messiah, the Son of David ([Isaiah 7:14](https://ref.ly/Isa7:14); [9:1–7](https://ref.ly/Isa9:1-Isa9:7); [11:1–16](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:16); [16:5](https://ref.ly/Isa16:5); [32:1](https://ref.ly/Isa32:1); [33:17–22](https://ref.ly/Isa33:17-Isa33:22)). He was a faithful king, but he was also human and would die. His failures and his sickness (chapters [38–39](https://ref.ly/Isa38:1-Isa39:8)) prepare the reader for the coming of the true Messiah described in chapters [40–66](https://ref.ly/Isa40:1-Isa66:24).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 38:1-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Hezekiah (ID: `person:Hezekiah`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`); Ahaz (ID: `person:Ahaz`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); David (ID: `person:David`); Word of the Lord (ID: `keyterm:WordOfTheLord`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Amoz (ID: `person:Amoz`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)

## Isaiah 38:3 (id: 16275)

*I have walked before You faithfully*: Hezekiah’s heart (his inner attitude and will) was right with the Lord for most of his reign ([2 Kings 18:1–5](https://ref.ly/2Kgs18:1-2Kgs18:5)). However, Hezekiah showed weakness when he relied on Egypt and did not act faithfully ([Isaiah 31:1–9](https://ref.ly/Isa31:1-Isa31:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 38:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hezekiah (ID: `person:Hezekiah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)

## Isaiah 38:5 (id: 16276)

*the God of your father David*: Hezekiah followed David's example with faithfulness (see [Isaiah 37:35](https://ref.ly/Isa37:35); [38:1–22](https://ref.ly/Isa38:1-Isa38:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 38:5

## Isaiah 38:6 (id: 16277)

*I will deliver you … I will defend this city*: This promise refers to saving Jerusalem ([Isaiah 36–37](https://ref.ly/Isa36:1-Isa37:38)). It also helps us understand Hezekiah’s illness and recovery. Just as Hezekiah gained 15 more years, Jerusalem also received a temporary relief (see [Isaiah 39](https://ref.ly/Isa39:1-Isa39:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 38:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`)

Hezekiah’s poem includes both lament ([Isaiah 38:9–14](https://ref.ly/Isa38:9-Isa38:14)) and praise ([38:15–20](https://ref.ly/Isa38:15-Isa38:20)). The praise remains restrained until the final verse.

## Isaiah 38:10 (id: 16267)

*go through the gates of Sheol*: This image comes from an ancient belief that people enter death through gates (see [Isaiah 14:9](https://ref.ly/Isa14:9)). Old Testament believers did not understand the afterlife as the New Testament describes it. They saw it as a shadowy place where all the dead gathered, and where no one praised God (see [Isaiah 38:18](https://ref.ly/Isa38:18); [Psalms 88:10](https://ref.ly/Ps88:10); [115:17](https://ref.ly/Ps115:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 38:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)

## Isaiah 38:11 (id: 16268)

Hezekiah was very sad when he thought he might die. He believed that death would separate him from fellowship with God. He also feared losing time with his family and friends.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 38:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 38:12 (id: 16269)

These images show how short human life is (compare [2 Corinthians 5:1](https://ref.ly/2Cor5:1)). They remind us that life on earth does not last long.

God’s restoration of Hezekiah’s health also symbolized the restoration from exile that Judah would experience in the future.

## Isaiah 38:21–22 (id: 16273)

Hezekiah received a sign from the Lord. This sign reminds us of another sign that the prophet had offered to Ahaz ([Isaiah 7:11](https://ref.ly/Isa7:11)). 

Ahaz and Hezekiah stand at the two ends of a long story in Judah’s history. Ahaz represents the ungodly king. Hezekiah represents the godly king. 

Ahaz brought disaster on his people through his choices. Hezekiah, in contrast, brought deliverance to his people. However, [Isaiah 39](https://ref.ly/Isa39:1-Isa39:8) shows that even Hezekiah’s story includes warning and future trouble.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 38:21-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`); Hezekiah (ID: `person:Hezekiah`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Fig Cake (ID: `realia:FigCake`)

## Isaiah 39:1 (id: 16280)

Merodach\-baladan, the king of Babylon from 722 to 710 and 704 to 703 BC, planned to rebel against Assyria. He sought help from Hezekiah. His envoys visited after Hezekiah recovered from a serious illness but before Sennacherib's final attack in 701 BC.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 39:1

*silver … precious oils … his entire armory*: By showing these treasures, Hezekiah demonstrated to Babylon's envoys that he had the resources to be a valuable ally.

## Isaiah 39:6–7 (id: 16282)

*carried off to Babylon*: This prophecy came before the prophecy about saving Jerusalem ([Isaiah 37:35](https://ref.ly/Isa37:35)). Although this judgment and exile seem like a surprising end to Hezekiah's story, the exile of Israel and Judah was always a possibility ([Isaiah 10:3–4](https://ref.ly/Isa10:3-Isa10:4), [20–23](https://ref.ly/Isa10:20-Isa10:23)). 

The Lord promised to protect Judah and Jerusalem from the Assyrians in 701 BC, but he warned Isaiah that worse would happen if they did not change. God showed his power and reliability by defeating the Assyrians, but the people did not change. The promises of [Isaiah 2:1–5](https://ref.ly/Isa2:1-Isa2:5) and [4:2–6](https://ref.ly/Isa4:2-Isa4:6) would only come true through judgment ([4:4](https://ref.ly/Isa4:4)). 

Until the exile cleansed the nation's wrongdoings ([Isaiah 6:5–7](https://ref.ly/Isa6:5-Isa6:7)), their mission to other nations would not succeed.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 39:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Isaiah 40:1–31 (id: 16290)

This chapter introduces chapters [40–66](https://ref.ly/Isa40:1-Isa66:24). In this section, comfort means the result of God's merciful actions toward people in the future. It includes encouragement, strength, and acceptance. 

God wants to rescue his people ([Isaiah 40:1–11](https://ref.ly/Isa40:1-Isa40:11)) and is fully capable of doing so ([40:12–26](https://ref.ly/Isa40:12-Isa40:26)). The exiles needed to trust God's promises and wait for their rescue ([Isaiah 40:27–31](https://ref.ly/Isa40:27-Isa40:31); see also [30:15–18](https://ref.ly/Isa30:15-Isa30:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:1-31

## Isaiah 40:1–66.24 (id: 16292)

The rest of Isaiah offers a message of comfort and reveals God's character and his plans for Israel. As [Isaiah 39:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa39:6-Isa39:7) predicted, Judah would face judgment and exile after Isaiah's time. Throughout [Isaiah 40–66](https://ref.ly/Isa40:1-Isa66:24), Isaiah prophesied as if the exile in Babylon had already occurred. Thus, the Babylonian exile is the context for understanding these chapters.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Bring Honor and Glory on Oneself (ID: `keyterm:BringHonorAndGloryOnOneself`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Delight (ID: `keyterm:Delight`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`)

## Isaiah 40:2 (id: 16310)

* *Speak tenderly*: The prophet's message was to encourage Jerusalem with the good news of God's forgiveness, reconciliation, and restoration.
* From the prophetic perspective, Israel’s sad days are gone because the exile has ended.
* *she has received from the hand of the LORD double for all her sins*: Israel faced the full force of God's wrath ([Isaiah 51:19–23](https://ref.ly/Isa51:19-Isa51:23); compare [61:7](https://ref.ly/Isa61:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Isaiah 40:3 (id: 16313)

* *A voice of one calling*: In [Malachi 3:1](https://ref.ly/Mal3:1), this person (possibly the Elijah of [Malachi 4:5](https://ref.ly/Mal4:5)) was God's messenger who prepared the way for God's coming. In the Gospels, people identified this announcer as John the Baptist ([Matthew 3:3](https://ref.ly/Matt3:3); [Mark 1:3](https://ref.ly/Mark1:3); [Luke 3:4](https://ref.ly/Luke3:4); [John 1:23](https://ref.ly/John1:23)).
* *Prepare the way … make a straight highway*: The announcer's message would speed up God's coming (see [Isaiah 35:8](https://ref.ly/Isa35:8)). As God made a way for Israel through the Red Sea ([Isaiah 43:16](https://ref.ly/Isa43:16)), a way would also be opened for the Lord (see [52:11–12](https://ref.ly/Isa52:11-Isa52:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); A Disembodied Voice (ID: `deity:Voice`)

## Isaiah 40:3–5 (id: 16311)

There is now a shift from announcing the good news to expecting God's arrival. The good news ([Isaiah 40:1–2](https://ref.ly/Isa40:1-Isa40:2)) is based on God himself. 

Isaiah described the Lord as coming on a highway through the desert (see [Isaiah 35](https://ref.ly/Isa35:1-Isa35:10)). However, the imagery is reversed here: In [Isaiah 35](https://ref.ly/Isa35:1-Isa35:10), the people traveled to Zion, toward the Lord. Here, the Lord is coming toward his people (see also [Isaiah 52:8](https://ref.ly/Isa52:8), where both images are combined).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:3-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); A Disembodied Voice (ID: `deity:Voice`)

## Isaiah 40:5 (id: 16314)

* The future display of God's glory would include Judah's return from exile but extend beyond that. The New Testament confirms that God's glory was revealed in Jesus Christ ([John 1:14](https://ref.ly/John1:14); [Hebrews 1:3](https://ref.ly/Heb1:3)).
* *the mouth of the LORD has spoken*: The promised salvation and judgment will surely happen because the Lord, who keeps his promises, has declared it (see [Isaiah 1:20](https://ref.ly/Isa1:20); [58:14](https://ref.ly/Isa58:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 40:6 (id: 16316)

* *Cry out*: So that everyone can hear.
* People are not worthy of the revelation of God’s glory because they are mortal.

## Isaiah 40:10 (id: 16293)

* The Lord's rule is different from that of unjust and weak rulers, whom he will judge. His rule is compassionate, just, righteous, and powerful.
* *His arm establishes His rule*: God showed his power in events such as:

    + the defeat of the Egyptians ([Isaiah 63:12](https://ref.ly/Isa63:12)),
        + the judgment of his enemies ([Isaiah 30:30](https://ref.ly/Isa30:30); [48:14](https://ref.ly/Isa48:14); [51:9](https://ref.ly/Isa51:9)), and
        + the rescue of his people ([Isaiah 59:16](https://ref.ly/Isa59:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 40:11 (id: 16294)

Isaiah used the well\-known biblical image of a shepherd to describe God's care for his people (see also [Isaiah 49:10](https://ref.ly/Isa49:10); [Psalm 23:1](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1); [Jeremiah 3:15](https://ref.ly/Jer3:15); [23:4](https://ref.ly/Jer23:4); [Ezekiel 34:11–17](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:11-Ezek34:17); [Matthew 2:6](https://ref.ly/Matt2:6); [John 10:1–18](https://ref.ly/John10:1-John10:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`)

## Isaiah 40:12–13 (id: 16295)

These rhetorical questions suggest the answer, “No one!” God alone created everything. He will judge creation ([Isaiah 24:17](https://ref.ly/Isa24:17)), and only he can and will restore it through his salvation.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:12-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Span (ID: `realia:Span`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Balance (ID: `realia:Balance`)

## Isaiah 40:12–17 (id: 16296)

In response to Israel's questions (spoken by the prophet), the Lord declared that he is the unmatched God, a wise and sovereign creator who governs the nations.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:12-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Balance (ID: `realia:Balance`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Span (ID: `realia:Span`)

## Isaiah 40:15 (id: 16297)

God rules over all nations and their power structures. Compared to God, they are almost nothing. This includes the Babylonian Empire, which was just a tool in God's hand. 

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Balance (ID: `realia:Balance`)

## Isaiah 40:16 (id: 16298)

People valued Lebanon for its plentiful wood and wildlife (see [Isaiah 2:13–16](https://ref.ly/Isa2:13-Isa2:16); [Psalm 104:16–17](https://ref.ly/Ps104:16-Ps104:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Lebanon (ID: `place:Lebanon`)

## Isaiah 40:17 (id: 16299)

The nations, including their leaders ([Isaiah 40:23](https://ref.ly/Isa40:23)), pagan structures ([41:24](https://ref.ly/Isa41:24),[29](https://ref.ly/Isa41:29); [44:9–11](https://ref.ly/Isa44:9-Isa44:11)), and all enemies of God's people ([41:11–12](https://ref.ly/Isa41:11-Isa41:12)), are worth nothing apart from God.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:17


## Isaiah 40:18 (id: 16300)

* *To whom … what image*: Earlier in the book, Isaiah showed that idolatry is foolish and empty ([Isaiah 16:12](https://ref.ly/Isa16:12); [37:16–19](https://ref.ly/Isa37:16-Isa37:19)). [Isaiah 40–48](https://ref.ly/Isa40:1-Isa48:22), he gives a much longer argument against it.

    An idol is an object that people make to represent a god or a religious idea. But these objects are made by human hands. Those who worship them do not see that they are only things people have made.

    Idols have no power ([Isaiah 41:7](https://ref.ly/Isa41:7), [22–24](https://ref.ly/Isa41:22-Isa41:24); [48:14](https://ref.ly/Isa48:14)). They give people a false feeling of safety ([Isaiah 42:17](https://ref.ly/Isa42:17)). They mislead people and cause deep disappointment ([Isaiah 44:20](https://ref.ly/Isa44:20); [42:17](https://ref.ly/Isa42:17); [45:16](https://ref.ly/Isa45:16), [20](https://ref.ly/Isa45:20)). They cannot save or protect anyone.

    In fact, idols are so weak that they can fall over ([Isaiah 40:20](https://ref.ly/Isa40:20); [41:7](https://ref.ly/Isa41:7)). They compete with God’s glory ([Isaiah 42:8](https://ref.ly/Isa42:8)). They also lower human dignity, because people bow down to things they have made ([44:9](https://ref.ly/Isa44:9); see also [41:21–29](https://ref.ly/Isa41:21-Isa41:29)).

* The Lord, the true God, is not like idols. No one can compare to him (see [Isaiah 40:25](https://ref.ly/Isa40:25); [46:5](https://ref.ly/Isa46:5); [Psalms 86:8](https://ref.ly/Ps86:8); [89:6](https://ref.ly/Ps89:6)). He is completely unique and without equal.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 40:20 (id: 16301)

*an idol that will not topple*: This is a sarcastic remark. It would be embarrassing if the idol could not even stand (see [Isaiah 40:18](https://ref.ly/Isa40:18); [41:7](https://ref.ly/Isa41:7); [1 Samuel 5:4](https://ref.ly/1Sam5:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mulberry (ID: `flora:Mulberry`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`); Craftsman (ID: `realia:Craftsman`)

## Isaiah 40:21 (id: 16302)

Idol worshipers do not recognize God or respond wisely to him. The faithful in Israel understand and see God's role in the story of redemption, which includes both exile and restoration ([Isaiah 41:20](https://ref.ly/Isa41:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Foundation (ID: `realia:Foundation`)

## Isaiah 40:22 (id: 16303)

* God rules as king over the entire earth (see [Isaiah 6:1](https://ref.ly/Isa6:1); [Psalms 2:4](https://ref.ly/Ps2:4); [80:1](https://ref.ly/Ps80:1); [99:1](https://ref.ly/Ps99:1); [102:12](https://ref.ly/Ps102:12); [113:5](https://ref.ly/Ps113:5)). God is so vast and powerful that humans seem like grasshoppers in comparison.
* Believing in God as the creator of the heavens and earth opposes the common Babylonian and Egyptian belief that the sun, moon, and stars are gods.
* *like a curtain...a tent*: See also [Psalm 104:2](https://ref.ly/Ps104:2).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Locust (ID: `fauna:Locust`)

## Isaiah 40:23 (id: 16304)

*princes*: God is not impressed by human power or fame. Human strength and reputation do not change who God is.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:23


## Isaiah 40:25 (id: 16305)

*The Holy One* is a shorter form of *the Holy One of Israel*. This is a common name for God in the book of Isaiah (see also [Isaiah 10:17](https://ref.ly/Isa10:17); [43:15](https://ref.ly/Isa43:15); [Psalm 22:3](https://ref.ly/Ps22:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 40:26 (id: 16306)

*Lift up your eyes on high*: Just as God told Abraham to look into the sky ([Genesis 15:5](https://ref.ly/Gen15:5)), he also told Israel to look to the stars for encouragement (see also [Isaiah 51:2–3](https://ref.ly/Isa51:2-Isa51:3)). The cosmos shows God's great power and unmatched strength. The Strong One can strengthen the weak ([Isaiah 40:27–31](https://ref.ly/Isa40:27-Isa40:31); see [Psalm 147:5](https://ref.ly/Ps147:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`)

## Isaiah 40:27 (id: 16307)

*My way is hidden … ignored by my God*: The coming exile, a time when God's powerful presence and rescue would not be visible, would prompt questions (see also [Isaiah 49:14](https://ref.ly/Isa49:14); [54:7](https://ref.ly/Isa54:7); [Lamentations 5:20–22](https://ref.ly/Lam5:20-Lam5:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 40:28 (id: 16308)

* *know … understood*: Compare [Isaiah 40:21](https://ref.ly/Isa40:21).
* *the everlasting God, the Creator of the ends of the earth*: Creation shows God's power and wisdom. He is also the Creator of the future world, a place of righteousness, justice, and peace ([Isaiah 45:8](https://ref.ly/Isa45:8); [48:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa48:6-Isa48:7); [65:17–18](https://ref.ly/Isa65:17-Isa65:18)).
* *He will not grow tired or weary*: God does not have human limitations.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`)

## Isaiah 40:29 (id: 16309)

In this context, the weak are those who face persecution or oppression, like during the exile in Babylon. The Lord is their only help (see [Isaiah 49:4–5](https://ref.ly/Isa49:4-Isa49:5); [50:4](https://ref.ly/Isa50:4); [Psalms 68:9](https://ref.ly/Ps68:9); [119:28](https://ref.ly/Ps119:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`)

## Isaiah 40:31 (id: 16312)

* *wait upon the LORD*: See [Isaiah 33:2](https://ref.ly/Isa33:2); [49:23](https://ref.ly/Isa49:23); [51:5](https://ref.ly/Isa51:5).
* *like eagles*: This strong image of rescue reminded readers of the exodus event from long ago ([Exodus 19:4](https://ref.ly/Exod19:4); [Deuteronomy 32:10–12](https://ref.ly/Deut32:10-Deut32:12); see also [Psalm 103:5](https://ref.ly/Ps103:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 40:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Vulture (ID: `fauna:Vulture`)

## Isaiah 41:1 (id: 16326)

* The nations gathering for a trial must listen quietly to God's arguments.
* *Let them come forward and testify*: Literally *let them find new strength,* which is a reference to [Isaiah 40:31](https://ref.ly/Isa40:31).
* God invited the nations to come and speak, challenging them to counter his case.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`)

## Isaiah 41:1–7 (id: 16321)

The Lord invited the nations to a trial to show that he alone is God. They could not defend themselves as he announced that a king from the east ([Isaiah 41:2](https://ref.ly/Isa41:2)) would defeat Babylon, allowing Israel to return.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:1-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Anvil (ID: `realia:Anvil`); Nail (ID: `realia:Nail`); Hammer (ID: `realia:Hammer`); Craftsman (ID: `realia:Craftsman`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`)

## Isaiah 41:2 (id: 16332)

* The king from the east was Cyrus, the Persian king who conquered Babylon in 539 BC. He allowed the Jews to return to their land and rebuild Jerusalem in 538 BC ([Ezra 1:1–4](https://ref.ly/Ezra1:1-Ezra1:4)).

    Isaiah’s prophecies about Cyrus ([Isaiah 44:28–45:13](https://ref.ly/Isa44:28-Isa45:13); [48:14–15](https://ref.ly/Isa48:14-Isa48:15)) encouraged Israel to see the Lord as the ruler over all human history, including the actions of great kings.

* *He hands nations over to him*: As victorious as the conquering Cyrus was, he was still subject to the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`)

## Isaiah 41:4 (id: 16333)

* *the generations from the beginning?*: God rules over everything. From the very beginning, he has guided each stage of history according to his plan.
* In the book of Revelation, Jesus calls himself *the Alpha and the Omega*, *the First and the Last*, *the Beginning and the End* ([Revelation 1:8](https://ref.ly/Rev1:8), [17](https://ref.ly/Rev1:17); [2:8](https://ref.ly/Rev2:8); [21:6](https://ref.ly/Rev21:6); [22:13](https://ref.ly/Rev22:13)).
* *I am He*: The Lord alone is God (see also [Isaiah 43:10](https://ref.ly/Isa43:10), [13](https://ref.ly/Isa43:13); [46:4](https://ref.ly/Isa46:4); [48:12](https://ref.ly/Isa48:12); [Deuteronomy 32:39](https://ref.ly/Deut32:39)). Jesus used similar expressions for himself (see [John 6:35](https://ref.ly/John6:35); [8:12](https://ref.ly/John8:12), [24](https://ref.ly/John8:24); [9:5](https://ref.ly/John9:5); [10:7](https://ref.ly/John10:7), [9](https://ref.ly/John10:9), [11](https://ref.ly/John10:11), [14](https://ref.ly/John10:14); [11:25](https://ref.ly/John11:25); [14:6](https://ref.ly/John14:6); [15:1](https://ref.ly/John15:1), [5](https://ref.ly/John15:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 41:5–7 (id: 16334)

This taunt against idol worship responded to God's raising up Cyrus. The nations hoped their idols would protect them.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:5-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Anvil (ID: `realia:Anvil`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Nail (ID: `realia:Nail`); Hammer (ID: `realia:Hammer`); Craftsman (ID: `realia:Craftsman`)

## Isaiah 41:6 (id: 16335)

*Be strong!*: The nations put their hope in things that have no strength, objects made by human craftsmen.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:6


## Isaiah 41:8 (id: 16337)

* The servant, representing the nation of Israel, was God's chosen one, with a purpose based on God's plans.
* *Abraham My friend*: In Hebrew, this phrase could mean *Abraham who loves me or Abraham whom I love.* (See also [James 2:23\)](https://ref.ly/Jas2:23).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`)

## Isaiah 41:8–16 (id: 16336)

These two prophecies about salvation for God's servant Israel ([Isaiah 41:8–13](https://ref.ly/Isa41:8-Isa41:13), [14–16](https://ref.ly/Isa41:14-Isa41:16)) prepare for the first of the suffering servant songs (see study note on Isaiah 42:1–4).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:8-16
* **Associated Articles:** Isaiah 42:1-4 (ID: `16339@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Worm (ID: `fauna:Maggot`); Threshing-Sledge (ID: `realia:Threshing-sledge`)

## Isaiah 41:9 (id: 16338)

* I have chosen you restates [Isaiah 41:8](https://ref.ly/Isa41:8) to emphasize God's commitment and faithfulness to the people of Israel, even though they had not been faithful to him.
* *not rejected you*: During the exile in Babylon, the people felt that God had rejected them (see [Isaiah 49:21](https://ref.ly/Isa49:21); [54:6](https://ref.ly/Isa54:6)). However, God had good plans for them.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:9


## Isaiah 41:10 (id: 16322)

* *I am with you*: God's promise of presence (see [Isaiah 7:14](https://ref.ly/Isa7:14)) is central to the Bible. Because God is present, his people do not need to fear (see also [Isaiah 43:1–2](https://ref.ly/Isa43:1-Isa43:2), [5](https://ref.ly/Isa43:5)).
* *I am your God*: The Lord uses covenant language to affirm that he is their God and they are his people (see also [Jeremiah 7:23](https://ref.ly/Jer7:23); [31:1](https://ref.ly/Jer31:1), [33](https://ref.ly/Jer31:33); [Ezekiel 14:11](https://ref.ly/Ezek14:11); [36:28](https://ref.ly/Ezek36:28); [37:27](https://ref.ly/Ezek37:27); [Zechariah 8:8](https://ref.ly/Zech8:8)).
* *uphold you with My righteous right hand*: The Lord uses language similar to the exodus (compare [Exodus 15:6](https://ref.ly/Exod15:6)) to encourage Israel (see also [Isaiah 41:13](https://ref.ly/Isa41:13); [63:12](https://ref.ly/Isa63:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Isaiah 41:14 (id: 16323)

* In exile, Israel was in a lowly state.
* God is the Redeemer. He works powerfully to rescue and restore people from sin and its consequences (for Israel, it was the exile; for an application to Jesus, see [Luke 2:38](https://ref.ly/Luke2:38); [21:28](https://ref.ly/Luke21:28); [Romans 3:24](https://ref.ly/Rom3:24); [1 Corinthians 1:30](https://ref.ly/1Cor1:30); [Galatians 4:5](https://ref.ly/Gal4:5); [Ephesians 1:1–14](https://ref.ly/Eph1:1-Eph1:14); [Titus 2:14](https://ref.ly/Titus2:14); [Hebrews 9:12](https://ref.ly/Heb9:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Worm (ID: `fauna:Maggot`)

## Isaiah 41:16 (id: 16324)

When the grain is thrown into the wind, the chaff is blown away. Likewise, Israel’s enemies appeared to be strong but would easily be driven off.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 41:18 (id: 16325)

*rivers … fountains* *…* *a pool of water* *…* *springs*: These pictures would have been very meaningful to people who lived by farming and raising animals. In an agrarian culture (a farming society), water meant life. Rivers, springs, and pools were signs of safety, blessing, and hope. These images also suggest that rescue from the exile in Babylon would be like a second exodus (compare [Exodus 15:27](https://ref.ly/Exod15:27); [17:6](https://ref.ly/Exod17:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:18


## Isaiah 41:21 (id: 16328)

*the King of Jacob*: See [Isaiah 6:5](https://ref.ly/Isa6:5); [43:15](https://ref.ly/Isa43:15); [44:6](https://ref.ly/Isa44:6).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 41:21–29 (id: 16327)

This trial scene develops the Lord’s case against idolatry. Idols are nothing but a human creation, whereas God is the Creator of all things. Idols cannot speak, act, accomplish anything, or save their worshipers.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:21-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Mischief (ID: `keyterm:Mischief`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Isaiah 41:25 (id: 16329)

* *I have raised up on*: The God who planned the exile through Babylon also planned Israel’s return from exile through Cyrus (see [Isaiah 41:2](https://ref.ly/Isa41:2); see also [13:17](https://ref.ly/Isa13:17)).
* Although Persia is to the east, travelers to Israel and Judah had to enter from the north due to rough terrain.
* *He will march over rulers … treads the clay*: Just as Babylon trampled Assyria, Persia would trample Babylon.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:25


## Isaiah 41:26 (id: 16330)

Idols and false gods could not predict the future.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:26


## Isaiah 41:27 (id: 16331)

The messenger with good news announced that God would come to save his people (see [Isaiah 40:9](https://ref.ly/Isa40:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 41:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Isaiah 42:1 (id: 16344)

* *in whom My soul delights*: God used similar words at the baptism of Jesus ([Matthew 3:17](https://ref.ly/Matt3:17)).
* *I will put My Spirit on Him*: Any leader might be called a servant, but the Spirit's presence suggests a king from David’s line or a prophet like Moses (see [Numbers 11:17](https://ref.ly/Num11:17), [24–29](https://ref.ly/Num11:24-Num11:29)).
* Establishing justice is a king's duty (see [Isaiah 9:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa9:6-Isa9:7); [Psalm 72:1](https://ref.ly/Ps72:1)).
* This king’s mission will extend beyond governing Judah to a greater mission for the nations.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`)

## Isaiah 42:1–4 (id: 16339)

The servant here is not the people of Israel (as in [Isaiah 41:8–28](https://ref.ly/Isa41:8-Isa41:28) and elsewhere in chapters [41–48](https://ref.ly/Isa41:1-Isa48:22); see [49:5–6](https://ref.ly/Isa49:5-Isa49:6)). Instead, the servant is a royal figure who accomplishes his mission with care for people, especially for those who are hurting. The passage is the first of four songs about this servant ([Isaiah 42:1–4](https://ref.ly/Isa42:1-Isa42:4); [49:1–13](https://ref.ly/Isa49:1-Isa49:13); [50:4–11](https://ref.ly/Isa50:4-Isa50:11); [52:13–53:12](https://ref.ly/Isa52:13-Isa53:12)). He brings in an era of universal justice. For the connection of the servant with Jesus Christ, see [Matthew 12:18–21](https://ref.ly/Matt12:18-Matt12:21).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Reed (ID: `flora:Reed`)

## Isaiah 42:2 (id: 16348)

*cry out or raise His voic**e*: The royal servant will speak with calm confidence in his message and calling from God (see also [Isaiah 11:1–5](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:2


## Isaiah 42:3 (id: 16349)

The royal servant will be gentle with the oppressed and discouraged (see [Isaiah 3:15](https://ref.ly/Isa3:15); [41:17](https://ref.ly/Isa41:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reed (ID: `flora:Reed`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)

## Isaiah 42:4 (id: 16350)

*the islands*: The nations want justice and guidance on how to achieve it. The servant is like Moses, who received the law. However, the servant is greater because he brings justice to all nations, not just Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`)

## Isaiah 42:6 (id: 16351)

* *I will keep you … to be a covenant for the people*: *The people* might refer to all the nations of the earth (see [Isaiah 49:6](https://ref.ly/Isa49:6)).
* *a light to the nations*: See also [Isaiah 51:4](https://ref.ly/Isa51:4); [Acts 13:47](https://ref.ly/Acts13:47).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gentiles (ID: `group:Gentile`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 42:7 (id: 16352)

The servant will help the spiritually blind see (see [Isaiah 6:10](https://ref.ly/Isa6:10); [29:18](https://ref.ly/Isa29:18)) and free spiritual captives from sin. This is in addition to freeing those held captive during the exile in Babylon (see [Isaiah 61:1](https://ref.ly/Isa61:1) with [Luke 4:18](https://ref.ly/Luke4:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Isaiah 42:8–9 (id: 16353)

*I am the LORD*: God created the world and only God can make predictions come true. God alone deserves glory and praise.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:8-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`)

## Isaiah 42:10–11 (id: 16340)

* Different geographical regions show the extremes of where people live. The entire earth is invited to praise God for his promise to save humanity.
* Kedar and Sela were examples of desert locations ([Isaiah 42:11](https://ref.ly/Isa42:11); see also [16:1](https://ref.ly/Isa16:1); [21:16–17](https://ref.ly/Isa21:16-Isa21:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:10-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kedar (ID: `person:Kedar`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sela (ID: `place:Sela`)

## Isaiah 42:13 (id: 16341)

* *a mighty one … a warrior*: This theme comes from God's victory at the Red Sea during the exodus ([Exodus 15:3](https://ref.ly/Exod15:3)).
* God will defeat all his enemies to save his people ([Isaiah 51:9](https://ref.ly/Isa51:9); [63:1–6](https://ref.ly/Isa63:1-Isa63:6); see also [Psalms 54:7](https://ref.ly/Ps54:7); [108:9](https://ref.ly/Ps108:9); [112:8](https://ref.ly/Ps112:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`)

## Isaiah 42:14 (id: 16342)

*I have kept silent … and restrained*: Israel felt God's absence during the exile in Babylon, which happened due to their idol worship ([Isaiah 57:11–13](https://ref.ly/Isa57:11-Isa57:13); [2 Kings 17:6–24](https://ref.ly/2Kgs17:6-2Kgs17:24)). The faithful will pray for God's presence and renewed involvement in their lives ([Isaiah 64:12](https://ref.ly/Isa64:12)), and the Lord will answer their prayers because he is committed to their redemption ([62:1](https://ref.ly/Isa62:1), [6](https://ref.ly/Isa62:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:14


## Isaiah 42:18–20 (id: 16343)

The Israelites who ignored God's words and actions became spiritually blind and deaf (see [Isaiah 6:9–10](https://ref.ly/Isa6:9-Isa6:10)). They knew the truth from God's word and the prophets, but their closed minds refused to respond.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:18-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`)

## Isaiah 42:21 (id: 16345)

*magnify His law and make it glorious*: God’s word is a witness to his righteous rule (see [Isaiah 1:10](https://ref.ly/Isa1:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`)

## Isaiah 42:22–25 (id: 16346)

Throughout its history, Israel's sins ([Isaiah 42:24](https://ref.ly/Isa42:24)) made it vulnerable to foreign oppressors. Many nations, especially Assyria and Babylon, became tools God used to express his anger ([Isaiah 42:25](https://ref.ly/Isa42:25)) toward his rebellious people.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:22-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Instruction (ID: `keyterm:Instruction`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Isaiah 42:25 (id: 16347)

Generation after generation of Israelites did not learn their lesson, even when God disciplined them through military defeat.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 42:25


## Isaiah 43:1 (id: 16358)

* *the LORD … who created you*: The idea of creation (see study note on Isaiah 40:28) now refers to God forming the nation of Israel.
* *He who formed you*: During the exodus, God freed his people from slavery. Similarly, God planned to bring his people back from exile to their land. Ultimately, Jesus gave his life as a ransom for all humanity ([Matthew 20:28](https://ref.ly/Matt20:28); [1 Timothy 2:6](https://ref.ly/1Tim2:6); [Hebrews 9:15](https://ref.ly/Heb9:15)).
* *I have called you by your name; you are Mine!*: Despite having faced his wrath, the people of Israel are still God’s chosen people.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 43:1
* **Associated Articles:** Isaiah 40:28 (ID: `16308@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Isaiah 43:1–7 (id: 16354)

In this promise of salvation, the Lord spoke directly to his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 43:1-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Bring Honor and Glory on Oneself (ID: `keyterm:BringHonorAndGloryOnOneself`); Seba (ID: `place:Seba`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 43:2 (id: 16363)

* Conquering forces are compared to flooding rivers (see [Isaiah 8:8](https://ref.ly/Isa8:8)).
* *I will be with you*: God promises to stay with his people to protect and care for them (see [Isaiah 7:14](https://ref.ly/Isa7:14); [41:10](https://ref.ly/Isa41:10); [43:5](https://ref.ly/Isa43:5); [45:14](https://ref.ly/Isa45:14)).
* Israel faced God's judgment as the fire of oppression during the exile in Babylon.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 43:2


## Isaiah 43:7 (id: 16364)

One of God’s purposes in restoring his people was to display his glory to the watching world.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 43:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Isaiah 43:8–13 (id: 16365)

The Lord here called on Israel, his blind servant, to be his star witness in a mock trial against idols and false gods. Israel knew that the Lord alone is God, and the people had experienced his salvation.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 43:8-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Believe In (ID: `keyterm:BelieveIn`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)

## Isaiah 43:10 (id: 16355)

*You are My witnesses*: By their very presence in exile, Israel was evidence that God is truly God. He predicted the exile long beforehand, and now it had come to pass. God alone is the true God because he speaks and acts and controls all of history. Idols and false gods could do none of these things. Israel would later become even greater evidence because God had also predicted their redemption ([Isaiah 43:11–12](https://ref.ly/Isa43:11-Isa43:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 43:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Believe In (ID: `keyterm:BelieveIn`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Isaiah 43:14–21 (id: 16356)

The Lord promised Israel that it would be freed from Babylon. This redemption would be similar to Israel's past freedom from Egypt but even greater in some ways ([Isaiah 43:18](https://ref.ly/Isa43:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 43:14-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Bring Honor and Glory on Oneself (ID: `keyterm:BringHonorAndGloryOnOneself`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Chaldeans (ID: `group:Chaldea`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`); Owl (ID: `fauna:Owl`); Ostrich (ID: `fauna:Ostrich`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`)

## Isaiah 43:18 (id: 16357)

God wanted the Israelites to remember the exodus from Egypt. However, they needed to focus on the amazing event that was about to happen, instead of staying stuck in the past.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 43:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Isaiah 43:21 (id: 16359)

One purpose of redemption is to honor God through the praises of the redeemed (see [1 Peter 2:9](https://ref.ly/1Pet2:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 43:21


## Isaiah 43:22 (id: 16360)

Israel's history of rebellion led them into many crises. Their rebellion was so strong that they refused to ask God for help. When they did pray, their wickedness often caused God not to hear their prayers ([Isaiah 1:15](https://ref.ly/Isa1:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 43:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Isaiah 43:25 (id: 16361)

God alone can blot out sins. This means he completely removes and forgives them.

It does not matter how many sins a person has committed or how serious they are. God has the power to forgive them all (see also [Isaiah 44:22](https://ref.ly/Isa44:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 43:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Isaiah 43:27 (id: 16362)

*spokesmen*: God held the kings of Israel and Judah especially responsible for leading the nation into sin (see [2 Kings 21](https://ref.ly/2Kgs21:1-2Kgs21:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 43:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Isaiah 44:1–5 (id: 16366)

The promise of salvation gave Israel a future. Even though the people had sinned, God would still use them to bless the next generation ([Isaiah 44:3](https://ref.ly/Isa44:3)). 

The past had been dark because of their sin (see [Isaiah 43:22–28](https://ref.ly/Isa43:22-Isa43:28)). But the future would be different. God would begin a new time of blessing. His Spirit would change the people and make this new beginning possible.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 44:1-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Poplar (ID: `flora:Poplar`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Willow (ID: `flora:Willow`)

## Isaiah 44:3 (id: 16375)

* The restoration theme is connected with God’s promise to pour out the Spirit (see also [Isaiah 32:15–17](https://ref.ly/Isa32:15-Isa32:17); [Joel 2:28–32](https://ref.ly/Joel2:28-Joel2:32); [Acts 2:16–18](https://ref.ly/Acts2:16-Acts2:18)).
* *on your descendants … your offspring*: The next generation of Israelites offered hope.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 44:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`)

## Isaiah 44:6–20 (id: 16376)

This passage uses the form of a trial speech to press God’s argument that disgrace comes to all who trust in idols and false gods.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 44:6-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`); Craftsman (ID: `realia:Craftsman`); Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Compass (ID: `realia:Compass`); Stone Pine (ID: `flora:Pine`); Chisel (ID: `realia:Chisel`); Stylus (ID: `realia:Stylus.2`); Laurel (ID: `flora:Laurel`); Elm (ID: `flora:Elm`); Cedar (ID: `flora:Cedar`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Measuring Reed (ID: `realia:MeasuringReed`); Oak (ID: `flora:Oak`); Hammer (ID: `realia:Hammer`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Isaiah 44:15–17 (id: 16367)

*He takes some of it to warm himself … He also fashions it into a god and worships it*: This description of making an idol is full of sarcasm and mocks the foolishness of idol worshipers (see also [Isaiah 44:19](https://ref.ly/Isa44:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 44:15-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`)

## Isaiah 44:18–19 (id: 16368)

*closed their minds*: Idol worshipers do not see the irony in using one part of a log for warmth and another part as their god.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 44:18-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Isaiah 44:21–22 (id: 16369)

The Lord promised to forgive the sins of Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 44:21-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`)

## Isaiah 44:23 (id: 16370)

All creation is called to celebrate God’s redemption of Israel (compare [Isaiah 1:2](https://ref.ly/Isa1:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 44:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Sandalwood (ID: `flora:Sandalwood`)

## Isaiah 44:24–45.8 (id: 16371)

The Lord alone is sovereign over history. He raised up Cyrus of Persia to execute judgment on Babylon and restore Israel to the Holy Land.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 44:24-45:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cyrus (ID: `person:Cyrus`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Practice Divination (ID: `keyterm:PracticeDivination`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Isaiah 44:25 (id: 16372)

False prophets and diviners used forbidden methods to predict the future. Eventually, God would reveal them as liars who believed something that is not true (see [Isaiah 3:2](https://ref.ly/Isa3:2); [9:15](https://ref.ly/Isa9:15); [47:13](https://ref.ly/Isa47:13); [Deuteronomy 18:10–11](https://ref.ly/Deut18:10-Deut18:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 44:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Practice Divination (ID: `keyterm:PracticeDivination`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`)

## Isaiah 44:27 (id: 16373)

The sudden loss of natural resources sometimes shows divine judgment (see [Isaiah 42:15](https://ref.ly/Isa42:15); compare [43:20](https://ref.ly/Isa43:20)). Here, however, God's ability to dry up streams with only a word shows his power to fulfill his promises through Cyrus of Persia.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 44:27


## Isaiah 44:28 (id: 16374)

*My shepherd*: Cyrus did more than just allow the Jews to return home. He led a major project to rebuild Jerusalem and restore the temple (see [Isaiah 45:13](https://ref.ly/Isa45:13); [Ezra 1:2–4](https://ref.ly/Ezra1:2-Ezra1:4); [6:3–5](https://ref.ly/Ezra6:3-Ezra6:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 44:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cyrus (ID: `person:Cyrus`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Isaiah 45:1 (id: 16381)

* *His anointed*: This title, usually for David or the Messiah, is used here for Cyrus. Cyrus was anointed because he was chosen for a special mission. No other foreign ruler received this title (compare [1 Samuel 10:1](https://ref.ly/1Sam10:1); [Romans 13:1](https://ref.ly/Rom13:1)).
* *whose right hand I have grasped*: The Lord would help Cyrus defeat Babylon (see [Isaiah 43:14](https://ref.ly/Isa43:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cyrus (ID: `person:Cyrus`); Anointed (ID: `keyterm:Anointed`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 45:3 (id: 16386)

*so that you may know*: Cyrus's victories show that the Lord can fulfill his plans. Idols and false gods cannot do this (see [Isaiah 41:21–29](https://ref.ly/Isa41:21-Isa41:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 45:4–5 (id: 16387)

*I call you by name*: Cyrus did not know the Lord, but the Lord knew him. Isaiah predicted the rescuer Cyrus by name 150 years before he was born. This prophecy would show the exiles that the Lord is truly God and that he knows the future.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 45:7 (id: 16388)

*light … darkness … prosperity … calamity*: Everything is under God’s control.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 45:9–11 (id: 16389)

*pot … the potter … him … his father*: It is foolish for a creature to question the wisdom of its creator.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:9-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 45:9–13 (id: 16390)

The Lord addresses those who doubt his decision to use a non\-believing king for his plans. As the Creator, he is free and has complete authority. He promised to return his people to their land and would use anyone he chooses to fulfill that promise.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:9-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Woe (ID: `keyterm:Woe.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Isaiah 45:14 (id: 16377)

*Egypt … Cush … Sabeans*: Israel's restoration would give them new sovereignty. Foreign nations would honor God. Recognizing God's blessing on His people, they would also honor them.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabeans (ID: `group:Sabean`); Sheba (ID: `person:Sheba`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`)

## Isaiah 45:17 (id: 16378)

Israel will never again face humiliation, disgrace, oppression, harassment, or abuse. These declarations suggest this promise will be fulfilled at the end of time.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Isaiah 45:18 (id: 16380)

Ancient pagan people saw the world as a place of empty chaos. However, God's plans are good. He created the world from the start to live in harmony with him.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 45:18–25 (id: 16379)

Salvation comes only from the Lord. He is the creator, the revealer, and the one who carries out his will in human history. He promised to start a new era of salvation and righteousness (see [Isaiah 45:8](https://ref.ly/Isa45:8)). Every person will submit to God, either by choice or not ([Isaiah 45:23–24](https://ref.ly/Isa45:23-Isa45:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:18-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Fairness (ID: `keyterm:Fairness`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`)

## Isaiah 45:22 (id: 16382)

* *Turn to Me … all the ends of the earth*: All people need to seek the Lord, the true God who created everything, while he is available ([Isaiah 55:6](https://ref.ly/Isa55:6)).
* In God there is salvation*,* the only hope for all people ([Isaiah 45:8](https://ref.ly/Isa45:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 45:23 (id: 16383)

* God's words are always true. When God swears by his own name, it shows he will never break his promise (see also [Isaiah 14:24](https://ref.ly/Isa14:24); [54:9](https://ref.ly/Isa54:9); [62:8](https://ref.ly/Isa62:8); [Hebrews 6:13](https://ref.ly/Heb6:13)).
* *Every knee … every tongue*: At the end of time, all nations will accept his authority, either willingly or unwillingly (see [Romans 14:11](https://ref.ly/Rom14:11); [1 Corinthians 15:25–27](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:25-1Cor15:27); [Philippians 2:10–11](https://ref.ly/Phil2:10-Phil2:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`)

## Isaiah 45:24 (id: 16384)

In this context, the people include everyone who seeks salvation from the Lord, even those from other nations ([Isaiah 45:22](https://ref.ly/Isa45:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 45:25 (id: 16385)

*justified*: Literally *righteous* (see also [Isaiah 45:8](https://ref.ly/Isa45:8), [24](https://ref.ly/Isa45:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 45:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 46:1 (id: 16396)

* *Marduk\-bel* was the most important god in the Babylonian group of gods. *Bel*, similar to *Baal*, meant lordship.
* Nebo (*Nabu,* *Nebu*), the patron god of Babylonian scribes, was believed to be the son of Marduk\-bel.
* Instead of being celebrated at a Babylonian festival, people would transport these gods on ox carts and discard them, as the Babylonian people rejected them.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 46:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bel (ID: `deity:Bel`); Nebo (ID: `deity:Nebo`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Isaiah 46:1–2 (id: 16391)

This taunt was against the self\-contradiction of Babylonian religion.

A taunt is a mocking song or speech. In this passage, the prophet makes fun of Babylon’s beliefs. The message shows that their religion does not make sense.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 46:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bel (ID: `deity:Bel`); Nebo (ID: `deity:Nebo`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Isaiah 46:3 (id: 16397)

* *the remnant of the house of Israel*: This refers to the survivors after Jerusalem's destruction in 586 BC.
* The Lord, the Creator of humanity, cares for and carries his people, unlike the Babylonian idols that need carrying.
* *sustained from the womb, carried along since birth*: The Lord chose Israel even before it became a nation (see also [Isaiah 49:5](https://ref.ly/Isa49:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 46:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Isaiah 46:8–13 (id: 16398)

Many exiles felt hopeless and doubted God's promise to rescue them. God addressed this doubt.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 46:8-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mighty One (ID: `keyterm:MightyOne`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Vulture (ID: `fauna:Vulture`)

## Isaiah 46:10 (id: 16392)

God knows the future before it happens*;* he controls all events, and his actions match his words ([Isaiah 41:4](https://ref.ly/Isa41:4), [26](https://ref.ly/Isa41:26)). He is the first and the last ([Isaiah 44:6](https://ref.ly/Isa44:6); [48:12](https://ref.ly/Isa48:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 46:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`)

## Isaiah 46:11 (id: 16393)

The swift bird of prey from the east refers to Cyrus, the Persian king (see study note on Isaiah 41:2).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 46:11
* **Associated Articles:** Isaiah 41:2 (ID: `16332@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vulture (ID: `fauna:Vulture`)

## Isaiah 46:12 (id: 16394)

Formerly, Israel cared little for doing right (literally *righteousness;* see also [Isaiah 58:2](https://ref.ly/Isa58:2); [59:9](https://ref.ly/Isa59:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 46:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mighty One (ID: `keyterm:MightyOne`)

## Isaiah 46:13 (id: 16395)

* The good news for Israel was that God was coming to make things right (literally *I will bring my righteousness near;* see [Isaiah 40:9](https://ref.ly/Isa40:9); [51:5](https://ref.ly/Isa51:5)).
* *adorn Israel with My splendor*: Unlike a craftsman who gives material splendor to an idol ([Isaiah 44:13](https://ref.ly/Isa44:13)), the Lord gives true dignity to his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 46:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Isaiah 47:1 (id: 16404)

To sit in the dust was a way of expressing humiliation.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 47:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Chaldeans (ID: `group:Chaldea`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Throne (ID: `realia:Throne`)

## Isaiah 47:1–4 (id: 16400)

In this taunt against Babylon, the nation is described as a proud queen.

But she is brought low and humiliated. Instead of ruling, she is forced to work like a servant girl.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 47:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Chaldeans (ID: `group:Chaldea`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Veil (ID: `realia:Veil`); Throne (ID: `realia:Throne`); Hand-Mill (ID: `realia:Hand-mill`)

## Isaiah 47:1–48.22 (id: 16399)

We can draw two conclusions from the earlier evidence that the Lord alone is God: 

1. Babylon's great pride is foolish ([Isaiah 47:1–15](https://ref.ly/Isa47:1-Isa47:15));
2. Israel should listen to the word of the Lord ([Isaiah 48:1–22](https://ref.ly/Isa48:1-Isa48:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 47:1-48:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Chaldeans (ID: `group:Chaldea`); Incantations (ID: `keyterm:Incantations`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Veil (ID: `realia:Veil`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Kiln (ID: `realia:Kiln`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Yoke (ID: `realia:Yoke`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`); Throne (ID: `realia:Throne`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Hand-Mill (ID: `realia:Hand-mill`)

## Isaiah 47:3 (id: 16405)

God will bring justice to the world by punishing those who oppose him and oppress his people (see [Isaiah 34:8](https://ref.ly/Isa34:8)). This will remove wickedness.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 47:3


## Isaiah 47:5–11 (id: 16406)

Babylon was known for its confidence and arrogance (see [Isaiah 14:13–17](https://ref.ly/Isa14:13-Isa14:17); [Daniel 4:30](https://ref.ly/Dan4:30)). The nation's charms, magic, and wisdom could not stop the disaster planned for it.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 47:5-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Incantations (ID: `keyterm:Incantations`); Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Chaldeans (ID: `group:Chaldea`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`); Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Yoke (ID: `realia:Yoke`)

## Isaiah 47:6 (id: 16407)

The people of Israel had provoked God’s wrath, prompting him to use Babylon to punish them. Now wicked Babylon itself would be punished.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 47:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Yoke (ID: `realia:Yoke`)

## Isaiah 47:8 (id: 16408)

* *I am, and there is none besides me*: This was a claim to divinity (compare [Isaiah 45:5](https://ref.ly/Isa45:5); [47:10](https://ref.ly/Isa47:10)). Only the Lord can make such a claim. It is extremely arrogant for any created thing, including a nation, to say this.
* Being a widow or losing one's children was considered a disgrace.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 47:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Safety (ID: `keyterm:Safety`)

## Isaiah 47:9 (id: 16409)

People often used witchcraft and magic to protect themselves from evil and ensure a happy future.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 47:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Incantations (ID: `keyterm:Incantations`)

## Isaiah 47:10 (id: 16401)

Here, *wisdom* and *knowledge* likely refer sarcastically to the false knowledge from occult practices. This false knowledge led Babylon away from the truth and into disaster ([Isaiah 47:11](https://ref.ly/Isa47:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 47:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Trust (ID: `keyterm:LeanOn`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`)

## Isaiah 47:12–15 (id: 16402)

When disaster struck, Babylon turned to occult practices for help, but found none.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 47:12-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Incantations (ID: `keyterm:Incantations`); Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`)

## Isaiah 47:13 (id: 16403)

Babylon was advanced in astronomy, which the astrologers and stargazers used as a form of divination (see [Daniel 2:2](https://ref.ly/Dan2:2), [10](https://ref.ly/Dan2:10); [5:7](https://ref.ly/Dan5:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 47:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Counsel (ID: `keyterm:Counsel`)

## Isaiah 48:1 (id: 16421)

*Jacob … Judah*: Isaiah usually focused on Judah, but here he spoke to all the tribes of Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:1

## Isaiah 48:1–2 (id: 16412)

Israel was religious but not truly committed to the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)

## Isaiah 48:1–11 (id: 16410)

This prophecy is a *disputation,* a speech in which the prophet argues a set of points against his audience.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:1-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`); Kiln (ID: `realia:Kiln`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`)

## Isaiah 48:1–22 (id: 16411)

The Lord urges Israel to abandon its long history of rebellion and pay attention to his words. In Hebrew, the word for *listening* often means the same as *obeying* or acting on what is heard.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:1-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Chaldeans (ID: `group:Chaldea`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Kiln (ID: `realia:Kiln`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Graven Image (ID: `realia:GravenImage`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Isaiah 48:3 (id: 16425)

The Lord had warned Israel about what was going to happen in the exile.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:3


## Isaiah 48:6–7 (id: 16426)

Although Israel often did not believe God's past predictions, God revealed new things they had not heard. Specifically, God revealed that Babylon would fall and Israel would be restored.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:6-7


## Isaiah 48:10 (id: 16413)

The exile in Babylon was a period of intense suffering (compare [Deuteronomy 4:20](https://ref.ly/Deut4:20)) that God used to purify Israel.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kiln (ID: `realia:Kiln`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`)

## Isaiah 48:11 (id: 16414)

*For My own sake … I will act*: Israel's exile made other nations question the Lord's character and reputation. These nations did not see that Israel's wickedness caused the exile. Instead, they saw it as a failure of Israel's God to protect his people and their land (see [Isaiah 36:19–20](https://ref.ly/Isa36:19-Isa36:20); [37:12](https://ref.ly/Isa37:12); [Ezekiel 36:19–26](https://ref.ly/Ezek36:19-Ezek36:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Isaiah 48:12–22 (id: 16415)

This final trial speech reviews the arguments of [Isaiah 40–48](https://ref.ly/Isa40:1-Isa48:22) and presents the final conclusion.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:12-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Chaldeans (ID: `group:Chaldea`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`)

## Isaiah 48:14 (id: 16416)

*chosen ally*: See [Isaiah 44:28](https://ref.ly/Isa44:28); [45:1–2](https://ref.ly/Isa45:1-Isa45:2).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Chaldeans (ID: `group:Chaldea`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`)

## Isaiah 48:15 (id: 16417)

*I have brought him, and he will succeed in his mission*: Cyrus would humiliate Babylon and liberate Israel ([Isaiah 45:1–4](https://ref.ly/Isa45:1-Isa45:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:15


## Isaiah 48:16 (id: 16418)

* *I have not spoken in secret*: God's promises are clear, though they do not always specify how or when they will happen.
* *And now the Lord GOD has sent me, accompanied by His Spirit*: The identity of the one sent is unclear. Isaiah might be referring to himself as a prophet inspired by the Spirit, speaking God's words to Israel. The connection with the spirit suggests it refers to the promised servant, the Messiah.
* The presence of the Spirit ensures the servant's success.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 48:18 (id: 16419)

* Peace and righteousness will be established when the kingdom of God fully comes (see [Isaiah 66:12](https://ref.ly/Isa66:12)).
* *like a river … like waves of the sea*: There will be abundant peace and overflowing righteousness (compare [Amos 5:24](https://ref.ly/Amos5:24)). Israel could have had this if its people had listened to God.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`)

## Isaiah 48:19 (id: 16420)

*Your descendants … as the sand*: This fulfills God's promise to Abraham (see [Genesis 22:17](https://ref.ly/Gen22:17)). In contrast, the Israelites were almost destroyed during the exile in Babylon (see [Isaiah 44:26](https://ref.ly/Isa44:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:19


## Isaiah 48:20 (id: 16422)

*Leave Babylon*: See also [Isaiah 52:11–12](https://ref.ly/Isa52:11-Isa52:12); [55:12](https://ref.ly/Isa55:12).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Babylon (ID: `place:Babylon`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Chaldea (ID: `place:Chaldea`); Redeem (ID: `keyterm:Redeem`)

## Isaiah 48:21 (id: 16423)

*not thirst … rock … water gushed out*: These reminders of God’s provision in the exodus provide a fitting summary of what he would do to rescue Israel from captivity in Babylon (see [Numbers 20:11](https://ref.ly/Num20:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:21


## Isaiah 48:22 (id: 16424)

This phrase, *There is not peace … for the wicked* (also [Isaiah 57:21](https://ref.ly/Isa57:21)), highlights the clear difference between the godly and the wicked (see [66:24](https://ref.ly/Isa66:24)). Peace represents God's kingdom, which does not include the wicked.

* **Associated Passages:** Isaiah 48:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Isaiah 49:1 (id: 16435)

* *O distant peoples*: The servant's work would not be limited to Israel. His ministry would reach people in faraway nations (see [Isaiah 42:4](https://ref.ly/Isa42:4), [10](https://ref.ly/Isa42:10), [12](https://ref.ly/Isa42:12); [51:5](https://ref.ly/Isa51:5); [60:9](https://ref.ly/Isa60:9); [66:19](https://ref.ly/Isa66:19)).
